## Phase 8: the improvement loop

**Visible output at 8.1.** The resolution counts appear on a run page that already exists.

**Finished here means built and proven over constructed and fixture data, and nothing more.** A verdict needs resolved setups and the store holds few: 93 under the entry rule 8.0 states, against a minimum of 250. Every done condition in this phase is met over data a checkpoint can construct, none reads the live store, and none waits on nights. The loop is built ready and stays silent until the evidence arrives, which is the guardrail working rather than a checkpoint left half done.

**A ruling taken at 8.2, which builds no checkpoint of this phase.** `CLAUDE.md` is read in full by every session before any work starts, and roughly three fifths of it is reference material only some sessions need. That material moves to `.claude/rules/`, four files each opening with a `paths` front matter block, which the agent loads when a session reads a file matching those paths and not before. Nothing is reworded and no rule changes: the text moves word for word, and the corpus checks read the four files as they read the one the text left, their populations widened first so that a citation leaving a fixed list does not stop being read with nothing going red. It is a ruling on an existing checkpoint rather than a checkpoint of its own, because the loop's numbers are fixed by the claims still out of scope at 8.3 to 8.6, and a checkpoint appended after the phase report would build nothing the report covers.

**This phase's pass carries repairs.** 8.0 rules on what phase 6 and the phase 7 sign-off left open, and several of those rulings are a line of code rather than a sentence, so the pass that plans this phase edits shipped source (see: The pass that plans phase 8 carries the phase 7 sign-off's repairs and its rulings' code). It builds no checkpoint of this phase, and the code it carries meets the done conditions a checkpoint's code meets (see: Code a pass that lands no checkpoint carries into shipped source meets the done conditions a checkpoint's code meets).

### 8.0 Planning
Confirms the guardrail values against the evidence that has accumulated. Nothing here is tuned to what the data turned out to be; a threshold changed after seeing results starts a new window and the old one is kept.

**Rules the fundamental analysis closed** (owes: The computed fundamental panel, and whether a fundamental state may fire a reason or gate a tranche). The four readings are not computed, no fundamental state fires a reason or gates a tranche, and no panel is drawn, a ruling superseded on 2026-09-27, when the quarters came to be fetched for every member on the night after it reports, so the four readings are computed for every member every night and the state they give orders tonight's list and gates nothing (see: Four readings of a member's reported quarters are worked out every night by rules the measured split settled, and its state is read from sales and operating margin alone). The mechanism is what decided it rather than taste: fundamentals arrive only when a name is opened, so a reason or a gate reading them either reads a store whose population is the names the operator happened to open, which makes two names in the same state carry different listing rows and leaves a shadow candidate unevaluated on most name-nights, or it fetches them nightly, which is the per-name request the nightly rule forbids. Section 4's numbers table already shows the reported quarters. The evidence is the fixture's captured filings and that code path rather than the operator's store, which holds no `fundamentals` row at all: the pass read it immutable on 2026-09-16 at 0 fundamentals, 0 source documents, 0 theme sections and 597 research sections, every one of them the key under each figure, over 495 names.

**Narrows what the volume profile is allowed to claim, before anything scores it** (owes: The volume profile's claim narrowed to a price region at a day's resolution). The claim is a price region at the resolution of a day's range and never a price within a session (see: The volume profile claims a price region at the resolution of a day's range). A session whose range exceeds three of its previous session's typical moves is kept in the profile and counted rather than set aside, so nothing the builder computes changes and no band moves. Measured over the 503 names that are members on 2026-09-14 and hold a profile as of it, being their last 60 sessions each less the 12 with no prior average true range, 85 of 30,168 sessions exceed three and 532 exceed two, 81 names hold one such session, 2 hold two and 420 hold none. The multiple is the event book's own shock multiple and moves with it (owes: The event setups' triggers calibrated from resolved setups).

**Rules the two things 6.11's production run found and left as they were.** A pass for a name with no facts file refreshes its industry's theme, because the theme is the industry's and every member reads it, and the name page says what the pass did instead of saying it did not run (owes: A name with no facts file refreshes its industry's theme, ruled) (see: A pass for a name with no facts file refreshes its industry's theme and says so). And a facts file carries the latest period of the segment table the archive supplies, twelve-month figures named by their months where the newest filing files no quarter, while a filing stored before the archive could be read keeps its parts unread until a newer filing arrives, which is stated as the limit it is rather than repaired by re-reading a stored filing (owes: The segment figures a facts file carries after an annual filing or an archive the fetch could not read) (see: A facts file carries the latest period of the segment table, and says which period it is). Over such a table the segment commentary is asked for the period its figures cover, and a figure held for that period alone is refused in a sentence naming a period of another length (see: A segment figure held for a period longer than a quarter is asked for by that period and refused where its sentence names a period of another length).

**Rules how a count a verdict note states is kept to the row it describes** (owes: A count a verdict note states read off the row it describes). A verdict note states no count of the row's own parts: what it says is what the check asserts, and the count lives in the row, which is the only place that cannot go stale (see: A verdict note states no count of the row's own parts). Read over all 346 notes the harness holds, 334 in the scope map and 12 in the placements, ten state such a count and each is rewritten without it, being the three matrix notes, four catalogue notes counting the stores a component declares, one catalogue note counting the feeds its component declares and two store notes counting SCHEMA's columns. Sixty-one others carry a number, and those are rule figures their row itself states or populations of the fixture, which the claim's own check reads. A guard refuses the shape from here, a count beside feeds among it.

**Decides what the momentum panel is for, rather than carrying it.** It is context a reader weighs, and no component decides anything from it: no reason, no band, no plan and no gate (see: The momentum panel is context a reader weighs, and nothing computes with it). Its neutral rules, being fifty on the relative strength index and zero on the histogram, are reading conventions the page draws and not thresholds any component applies, so they are neither pinned constants nor figures a measurement settles. The one reader outside the name page is the facts file, which copies every indicator the engine wrote, so prose may state a reading and section 5's convention for it and may never present one as a signal. The property is asserted by behaviour rather than by a scan on names: the fixture is replayed through the indicator engine once, one name's momentum rows are changed without re-running it, and the stages that follow are run again, with the level, ladder and listing rows asserted byte for byte identical and that name's facts file asserted to differ. Giving the panel a job later is a candidate like any other, registered at 8.3 and scored in shadow at 8.4.

**Rules the two conventions the loop's arithmetic rests on, rather than opening rows that wait on nothing.** The family's maximum size and the significance level are conventions, and no count of resolved setups measures either: the arithmetic that sets them is the one section 13 already states, where five worthless candidates clear an ordinary test about 23 per cent of the time and twenty clear it about 64. Both are decided here with that reasoning, and section 17's rows state decided values (see: Each setup family's correction for luck counts its own rules alone, at most nine a family) (see: A verdict tests a reason's wins against each of its setups' own break-even at a corrected threshold). The version bound is the one figure of the three a measurement settles, and it opens an operating row, because the scorer's own nights are what time it (owes: The rule version bound set from nights the version scorer ran).

**States what the loop scores, from the store as it stands.** Under the entry rule 8.1 builds, the setup rows the store holds resolve as 42 wins, 51 losses and 105 never entered, against 147 wins and 51 losses as the filler wrote them, over 2,522 listings on seven sessions read on 2026-09-16. Every one of those figures is re-read at the checkpoint that uses it rather than carried from here.

**Settles what the corpus says about a tranche zone's edges.** The trace this pass ran over the 223 zones that fired on 2026-09-15 found the merge rule holding: no band's neighbouring anchors sit more than half a typical day's move apart, and the widest gap is exactly half. It also found a moving average setting 36 of those zones' 446 edges, where the decision on averages says they do not decide where to buy and section 17's eligibility row says a band keeps its full width. The two are reconciled in words, and nothing the builder computes changes (see: A moving average may widen a band that a tranche sits on, and may never anchor one). The half that says never an anchor is enforced in `LadderSeries`, which keeps only bands carrying a non-average anchor and names its refusal, and `ABandAnchoredOnAnAverageAloneCarriesNoTrancheAndIsStillAStop` holds it over a constructed band set, so this pass names that test rather than writing a second. The size of the question is measured rather than argued: an average widens 35 of the 223 zones and 3 of them would no longer hold the close if a zone were the range of its non-average anchors alone, being APO, GRMN and NDAQ on that night. The alternative is not decided by taste here: it is registered at 8.6 as the named rule version `zone edges from non-average anchors only`, which is what a ladder rule change is scored as. Leaving the two sentences in tension was the third option and is not taken, because phase 8 would then be scoring the zones they disagree about.

**Carries the phase 7 sign-off's items, each closed here rather than entered.** The candidate register starts at one checkpoint, 8.3, where the migration creates it, so the roster row, the three placements, the two comments and the page's own line say 8.3 or the checkpoint that builds what they name. `two-platform` reads every entry written since the hosted Windows leg was removed, whatever it lands, split out of the record by the reader `DuePoints` lands checkpoints from, so no entry carries code that owes no Windows record (see: Every entry written since the hosted Windows leg was removed records the Windows run, whatever the entry lands). The started-phase guard is derived from the plan and the record rather than floored at five, as the exact set: every phase before the newest whose opening has landed has started and landed, and that newest one alone may be planned and not started. A `--session` older than the newest session the store holds is refused at the argument, since the corporate action refetch on that path drops a due name's newer bars, which this pass traced. The two thin tests are widened: the weekly retry's midnight case pins tonight's session as well as the last asked, and a suspect name the index no longer holds is read on all three of its surfaces. The wording the phase left behind is corrected in `BUILD_PLAN.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `RUNBOOK.md`, with the prior text in `CHANGELOG.md`.

**The claims phase 8 predicts.** 351 claims and 335 PASS after this pass, with 16 out of scope, 0 unexamined and 0 fail, which is where the 5.4 correction left them: this pass adds no claim and moves none into or out of scope. The 16 stand at 1 at 8.1, 4 at 8.3, 1 at 8.4, 9 at 8.5 and 1 at 8.6 once the family size row moves to the checkpoint that builds the register. After the loop is built the pair is 361 and 361 with 0 out of scope, and every claim it adds is named at the checkpoint that adds it.

**Done when** every row due at 8.0 is discharged in the carried obligations table and none is left open at a checkpoint the record shows as landed, the register's start names 8.3 everywhere it is named, no due point, placement, roster row or line on a page names a checkpoint this phase does not have, section 20's row and this section agree on what the loop builds, and the pair above is stated.

### 8.1 Setup resolution from the entry
The setup horizon on forward returns, counted from the entry rather than from the listing. `ForwardReturnSeries` has resolved a setup since 5.5 by walking the closes after a listing for a stop closed through or a target reached inside a cap of 63 sessions, and it counts a target reached as a win whether or not the price ever came back to the entry zone. Over the operator's store on 2026-09-16 that is 105 of 147 stored wins.

A setup starts on the first close at or below its entry zone's top edge. A target reached before that close is `never entered`, which is its own value and its own column, never a win and never in a rate, for the reason an unresolved setup is never one (see: A setup is scored from its entry, and a target reached before the entry is never a win). A first close that is at or below the zone's top edge and through the stop is entered and stopped on that session, so a fall through the whole plan is a loss rather than a non-trade. A setup that neither enters nor sees its target inside the cap is never entered, and one that enters and reaches neither is unresolved. The cap is counted from the listing night, because the plan being scored is the one stored that night and it ages with it, so an entry late in the cap has less of it. `return_pct` for this horizon is measured from the entry close, and SCHEMA says so.

The filler rewrites the setup outcomes it has already written, once, and the checkpoint's entry states the counts before and after over the same population. It is the one rewrite: no verdict has been shown and none can be, since the minimum is 250 and the store holds 93 resolved, and an outcome once decided is never written again (see: An outcome once decided is never rewritten, and a setup still in play is scored with its plan scaled by its listing session's adjustment factor).

**Done when** a win, a loss, an unresolved setup, a setup never entered, a setup never entered by the cap, a gap through the zone and the stop on one session, and a late entry that reaches the cap each have a test; never entered and unresolved are counted in their own columns and never in a rate; the counts render on the run page; and the rewrite's counts are recorded.

### 8.2 The break-even score
The hit rate each plan demanded, computed from its own entry close, stop and target, and the share of setups that beat it. It is stored beside the setup's own row, because the row is what the page reads and what a later session scores.

The figure crosses from prices in decimal to a statistic in double, so it goes through a named crossing helper and `price-storage-form`'s stated set of crossing sites gains it.

**Done when** the arithmetic is asserted against worked cases, including section 13's own risk of 65 against a reward of 137 at about 32 per cent, and a verdict below the minimum is withheld with its count shown against the minimum, over a constructed store rather than over whatever the live one holds.

### 8.3 The candidate register
Migration creating `candidate_register`, append only. Registration before scoring, with the rule, the test and the date.

A registered candidate names an evaluator the code carries and the parameters it is evaluated with, so the register says what will run rather than describing it. The evaluator carries its own version, a test pins that version to the pin of the evaluator's source and every source its evaluation runs through with line endings normalised and any leading byte order mark removed, and a candidate whose evaluation has moved on is a new registration rather than an edited row (see: A registration names an evaluator the code carries, and its version is the pin of every source its evaluation runs through but the catalogue). A row registered after a night's start is not evaluated by that night.

The family is at most nine and the correction divides the threshold by the candidates standing before the window opened, a name retired and registered again once (see: Each setup family's correction for luck counts its own rules alone, at most nine a family) (see: A candidate stands by the last row naming it, and a name retired and registered again stands once).

**Done when** an update, a delete and a replace are each refused at the store, a retirement is a new row naming what it retires, the correction divisor matches the candidates standing before the window opened with a name retired and registered again counted once, an evaluator whose evaluation's sources moved without its version fails, and `register-append-only` runs on every CI run from here.

### 8.4 The shadow column
Registered candidates evaluated nightly on every name-night exactly as live reasons are, written to the shadow column.

What a screen may show is the decision as it stands: a shadow evaluation of a name reaches no screen, and the run page's region states how many candidates are registered and that their records are withheld until one is promoted (see: Candidate conditions are registered before they are scored, and a candidate's picks are shown on the Run page while its outcomes wait for a look). A candidate whose evaluator version no longer matches the code is not evaluated and is named as a failure on the run log, because a shadow candidate has to be evaluated on every name-night and a note nobody reads is not a record of having skipped one. A name-night the night holds no readings for, being a stale or gapped name or a reading not available, is skipped on its own row and counted on the stage's line, and is not a failure (see: Only a missing or moved evaluator fails the shadow column, and a name-night without the readings is a counted skip). No component this phase builds computes a candidate's record, so the region withholds a record nothing computes and no candidate can be promoted until one does (owes: A candidate's shadow record computed, and a promotion written against it).

**Done when** a shadow candidate is evaluated on nights no live reason fired, which is what the every-name listing row exists for, a candidate registered after a night's start is not evaluated by it, and no screen carries a shadow evaluation of a name.

### 8.5 Reason verdicts on the run page
Each reason against the bar its own setups demanded, with the resolved count and the divisor beside it, and the base rate pinned.

A verdict is a one-sided test of the reason's wins against each of its setups' own break-even, at 0.05 divided by the family the reason belongs to (see: A verdict tests a reason's wins against each of its setups' own break-even at a corrected threshold). The six live reasons are one family of six, registered by section 11 before the first listing night; registered candidates are the other, at most nine; and a candidate promoted to live joins the live family, which restarts its window.

Below the minimum nothing is shown but the count against it, and the minimum is 250 resolved setups that set a bar, spread over at least 60 distinct listing sessions, each contributing at least one (see: A reason's share, verdict and both floors are counted over the resolved setups that set a bar). Both figures are ruled with the verdict rather than proposed: sixty is the quarter of trading the bands look back over and the threshold calibration already uses, and a count of rows alone can be filled by a handful of nights of one market move.

**Done when** no verdict appears below either minimum, every verdict names its divisor, the three display states are each proved over constructed counts, and the exact test matches hand-worked cases.

### 8.6 Rule versions scored counterfactually
Each ladder rule a named version, every night scored under every version from stored bars.

The rules with versions are the merge distance, where the stop sits, the near-exit skip and, from 8.0's ruling on a tranche zone's edges, `zone edges from non-average anchors only`. A version change opens a new window and keeps the previous one, and a live rule whose parameters or code have moved while a window measuring it is open stops the night at this step and names the rule, because a measurement whose subject moved says nothing about either version.

A version added later is not scored over the nights before it, since a rule written after those nights were seen and then scored on them is measured in sample; a backfill may store such scores flagged as in sample and they count toward no record and no verdict.

The bound is at most two windows of the merge distance and four of each other rule, each rule's live window among them, which is eighteen at once, settled by the operator on 2026-09-28, and a version is opened only beside its rule's live window (see: A ladder rule's version is measured beside that rule's live window, and both count against a bound of eighteen). Its arithmetic is stated: the night of 2026-09-14 took 495 seconds over the steps before the close, its level stage 143 seconds and its ladder stage 5 at 504 names, so a merge distance version costs a level and a ladder replay and every other version costs a ladder replay, and the fullest register the caps admit replays one of the first and twelve of the second, which puts the night at 703 seconds against the deadline of 7200. The arithmetic grows with versions times names and never with a network request, and `nightly-cost` asserts that over a recorded night at one version and at the fullest register (owes: The rule version bound set from nights the version scorer ran). A window is opened and closed through the worker's `version` verb, never by a night.

**Done when** a version change opens a new window and keeps the previous one, a live rule that moved inside an open window stops the night naming the rule, a backfilled score counts toward no record, and the bound refuses the fifteenth version.

### 8.7 Phase 8 report
The phase report over phase 8, with the pair 8.0 predicted checked against the actual and every claim that moved named. Section 13.2's and 13.3's placements fall due here, each converted to the check that reaches it, with every one of section 13.3's guardrails mapped to the test that holds it.

The model's own proposal was the loop's eighth checkpoint until 8.0 dropped it (see: The model writes no entry or exit proposal, and the loop scores only what code computes), which is why this phase has seven and section 13.2 has three rows rather than four.

**Done when** every guardrail has its own test, the loop has changed nothing on the strength of evidence below its stated minimum, asserted over the register's rows and the version windows rather than stated, and the pair 8.0 predicted is checked against the actual.

## Phase 9: asking for a report from the list

**Visible output at 9.1.** Each row of tonight's list says whether the name holds research, on a page that already exists.

**Opened before phase 8's sign-off, on the operator's ruling of 2026-09-20** (see: The report generation the operator asked for is built as phase 9, and phase 8's sign-off is owed after it rather than before it). That sign-off stays owed on phase 8 as a whole and nothing in this phase discharges it. The ruling moves the order of the two and nothing else.

**What this phase is for.** A name's own page can already ask for a pass and watch it. Four things it cannot do are what the operator asked for on 2026-09-20: ask from the row on tonight's list rather than from the name's page, make a request that outlasts the press, read what is queued on a screen of its own and take a report out of the queue that has not been written yet, and say which lane would write one. The correction at 6.5 is what makes the first of them worth drawing, because until it landed no name was ever missing its research and the control was never offered.

**The queue is the only mechanism, and both surfaces feed it.** The operator asked that queueing happen from tonight's list, and was asked what becomes of the control 6.5 made reachable on every name page. They ruled that it stays and queues as the list does: nothing starts a process any more, both surfaces write the same request, and the queue screen shows either. So "only from the list" is read as the queue being the only path rather than the list being the only button, and no surface keeps a second way to start a pass.

### 9.0 Planning
Writes this phase's section of the architecture, its checkpoints and their done conditions, and the decisions the phase rests on, the queue superseding the process started beside the read surface among them. Builds none of it.

**Done when** section 15.7's regions name what a row says about research and what it can ask for, section 15.15 states the queue screen and what a reader takes out of it, section 15.1 names the five screens and where each sits, every claim these add is placed at the checkpoint that ends it and reads as out of scope until then, and every decision the four checkpoints below cite resolves.

**The request store is declared at 9.2 and not here.** A planning pass could write the table and its ownership row now, and `writer-ownership` would refuse it in the direction that reads the code: a writer declared before its component exists is a declaration with nothing behind it. This file has been bitten by that twice, at 4.0 when the computed tables' deleters were declared ahead of the code and at 5.0 when a column was declared ahead of its migration, and `move` waited for 5.2 for the same reason. So 9.2 writes the table, its three operations and their two components together with the migration that creates it.

**The decision the queue supersedes is superseded at 9.2 and not here.** The control starts the worker's verb as a process of its own until 9.2 replaces it, and that is what the shipped source does and what six citations point at. A decision moved to Previously decided while the code it describes is still running would make the corpus describe a mechanism nothing had built, and would fail `no-superseded-citation` on every one of those six. So 9.2 writes the replacement, moves the old entry with its reasoning, and repoints the citations in the same commit.

**The queue screen is numbered 15.15 and not 15.11.** The screens are 15.7 to 15.10 and what follows them is not, so a fifth screen belongs among them by subject. It is appended instead, because inserting it would shift 15.11 to 15.14 by one and the record already cites those numbers, and a heading number that moves repoints every entry that named it with nothing going red. Section 15.1 names the five screens and where each sits, so the grouping is read off a list rather than off the numbering.

### 9.1 The report state on tonight's list
Each row says whether the name holds research, and the list stops calling a link to a name a report where none was written.

**Done when** every drawn row states whether the name holds an accepted section besides the key under each figure, read back off the page against the store in both directions, so neither a row claiming research the store lacks nor a row hiding research it holds passes; the night header's count of reports carrying prose is counted over researched sections rather than over every stored section, with the two counts shown to differ over a store where they do; and the words the link is drawn with are asserted against what the store holds for that name rather than against the route it points at.

### 9.2 The request queue
A store table both surfaces write a request to and the worker drains, replacing the process started beside the read surface. The name page's control and the list's ask write the same row.

**Done when** a press on either surface writes one request row and starts no process, asserted over the shipped source, which holds no process start for a pass at all, and over both hosted routes; the worker drains the queue oldest first and writes one run per request under that request's own identifier; a second request for a name whose request is outstanding is refused and the row says so; a request for a name whose pass already wrote something that day is refused, counted over passes that wrote rather than over passes that ran; the table is declared in `SCHEMA.md` with a writer named for each of its three operations and passes `writer-ownership` in both directions; and a request whose worker never ran still reads as outstanding rather than as lost, asserted over a store where the drain did not run.

### 9.3 The queue screen, and taking a report out of it
A fifth screen in the masthead, reading what is outstanding, what is being written now and what finished. A request that has not been written yet is withdrawn from it.

**Done when** the screen states every request the store holds with the state it is in, read back off the page against the store in both directions so neither a request the store lacks nor one it holds and the page omits passes; a request that has not been claimed is withdrawn by a press and the row says who withdrew it and when; a withdrawal is refused once the worker has claimed the request, because what the operator asked to remove is a report that has not been generated, and the refusal says which state refused it; nothing is deleted, so a withdrawn request is still read as having been asked for; and the withdrawal is asserted on the surface a person reads rather than only through the route.

### 9.4 The report generation lane, stated and half refused
The head of the page states which lane would write a report, as the operator's two words rather than a model's name. The local choice is drawn and refused until the comparison is measured.

**Done when** the lane is drawn in the operator's own words and carries no model name (see: Every part of a page states where it came from and as of when, and a written section when it was written rather than which model wrote it); the local choice is drawn, is not selectable, and states what it waits on, asserted on the surface a person reads rather than in the code that draws it; the paid choice writes a request as 9.2 leaves it; and which lane a request carries is read off configuration at the press rather than chosen per request, so a queue drained a day later writes under the lane the press meant.

## Phase 10: tonight's list as a selection worth buying

**Visible output at 10.1.** Tonight's list breaks a tie in fired count by the plan's reward to risk rather than by band strength, and a row with no reward to risk says why.

**What this phase is for.** The operator ruled at 9.4 that the list exists to select the stocks worth buying or paying attention to that evening (see: Tonight's list selects the stocks worth buying or paying attention to that evening, and the improvement loop exists to make that selection better). As built, the loop cannot select: phase 8 built the candidate register and the shadow column and nothing that judges a candidate, and the six live reasons fire on 65 to 86 percent of the index. This phase builds the judging, registers three tightened conditions as candidates, versions the trend rule, and changes one thing a reader sees, the tiebreak. It cannot show that the list picks well, because that is evidence the nights produce: a candidate is judged only at looks at 8, 12 and 16 blocks of 63 sessions, the first of them 504 sessions away and unable to promote, and the earliest promotion 756 sessions away (see: A candidate's verdict is read only at looks fixed when it is registered, with each look's boundary found over every sign vector its blocks allow).

**Only two changes reach a screen before a promotion.** The tiebreak at 10.1, and the one statement of twenty drawn, which 10.0 writes. Every condition this phase adds is a candidate or a rule version, registered and scored in shadow, and moves nothing a reader sees until it is promoted on its record (see: Candidate conditions are registered before they are scored, and a candidate's picks are shown on the Run page while its outcomes wait for a look).

**The ordering decision is superseded at 10.1 and not here.** "Tonight's list orders by the reasons that fired and then band strength, which are facts about the chart" describes what the shipped source does until 10.1 changes it, and a decision moved to Previously decided while its code still runs describes a mechanism nothing has built, as 9.0 said of the queue. So 10.1 writes the tiebreak decision, moves the old one with its reasoning, repoints its citations and edits section 11.1 and section 15.7 in the same commit.

**Section 17's rows for this phase land with their constants.** `pinned-constants` fails a figure section 17 states in digits that no code constant holds, so a row written here for the block length, the block minimum, the looks, the cost or the rule version cap would fail until the checkpoint that creates the constant. Each lands at that checkpoint: the block length 63 and the floor of 8 blocks at 10.1, whose run page region is the first thing to count blocks, the looks at 8, 12 and 16 and the cost of 10 basis points at 10.2, and the cap of 18 at 10.4. Until then section 13.6 states each number with its derivation and what it rests on.

**The loop's missing half is discharged at 10.2.** "A candidate's shadow record computed, and a promotion written against it" stays an operating row until 10.2 builds what it asks for and marks it discharged; its trigger fires the night the three candidates are registered, which is after 10.3.

### 10.0 Planning
Writes the decisions this phase rests on, section 11.1's selection procedure as built, section 13.6's verdict procedure, section 10.1's trend versions and this section. Builds none of it.

**Done when** every decision the checkpoints below cite resolves, every rule section 10.1, 11.1 and 13.6 add carries what it rests on, section 11 states twenty drawn once and no longer says the twenty lasts until the thresholds are calibrated, every claim these add is placed at the checkpoint that ends it and reads as out of scope until then, and every operating row below states a numeric trigger, the surface it is read on and the checkpoint that builds that surface.

### 10.1 The tiebreak and the twenty drawn
Tonight's list orders by how many reasons fired, then by the reward to risk the night's own plan computes from its first tranche, higher first, then by ticker. A row with no traded tranche sorts after every row with one in its fired-count group and says why, in the plan's own words. The tonight route, the name page's walk and every oracle read the plan of the night shown, off the plan the listing kept that night. Each listing records the highest strength of the name's bands, because bands and plans are dropped a year back and the comparison reads years of nights. A run page region measures three orders over the twenty each would draw, the old order as the benchmark, in blocks of 63 sessions against a floor of 8 (see: The order tonight's list is drawn in is compared against the order it replaces, declared before any record is read) (see: A listing records the band strength the old order read, and the three orders are compared over the nights that recorded it).

**Done when** the order is read back off the page against the store in both directions, a row with no reward to risk is drawn last in its group with its reason, the fixture gains a second night replayed from its committed year of bars at the session of 2026-09-03 with the overnight queue not run for it, so no model recording is keyed on it, and both nights' orders are derived by hand in the listings expectation (owes: An earlier night's order on tonight's list asserted from the fixture), the ordering decision is superseded with its citations repointed, every listing the night writes records its band strength, section 17 carries the block length and the block floor with their constants, and the region names its benchmark, its counts agree with constructed nights worked by hand either side of a block edge and of a closed window, and it shows no comparison before its block count is reached (owes: The three orders of tonight's list compared).

### 10.2 A candidate judged, and retired or promoted
The record a registered candidate is judged by: the block sign-flip test over closed-window setups on a calibrated null, with the Poisson-binomial beside it; the looks, their spending and the Holm graph; the futility guideline and the maximum look; the monitoring figure and the verdict field; the design effect, realized loss, same-session losses and earnings stop-outs reported; and the promotion and the retirement written down as procedures in `RUNBOOK.md` (see: A candidate is judged by a sign-flip test over blocks of 63 sessions, with at least eight blocks). It is what the loop's missing half asked for, so it discharges it (owes: A candidate's shadow record computed, and a promotion written against it).

**Done when** over a constructed register and shadow column worked by hand, a sign-flip p-value agrees with a hand count of sign vectors, including a case where it and the Poisson-binomial disagree; fewer than 8 blocks are withheld; a permutation of setups returns 1; a first look at 8 blocks cannot cross at the ruled level; a look reads only closed-window setups and a nightly read never changes the verdict field; the graph passes a promoted level as stated and a retired level to no one; a same-session loss is counted with its null; the calibration's method, inputs and cap are pinned; section 17 carries the looks and the cost with their constants; the record region states each look's power and the proposed numbers each candidate was registered with (owes: A candidate's power re-read at each look) (owes: The candidates' proposed numbers read at their looks); and no candidate's record reaches a screen beside a ticker.

### 10.3 The three candidates
`CandidateNight` carries what the three read: the previous close, the nearest zone's edges, the typical move, the volume, the fifty-day average, the night's median ratio from a pre-pass that makes no request, and the crossing distances. The evaluators are `arrived-and-narrow`, `volume-against-the-night` and `crossed-by-a-margin`, each registered by one `RUNBOOK.md` command run once by the operator, all three at one instant (see: The three candidates are registered at one instant).

**Done when** each evaluator matches the fixture's own night worked by hand and constructed input at its edges, a close that fell through the zone, a close already inside it, a zone exactly a typical move wide, a quiet night whose median is below 1 and a close exactly at the margin among them; a night missing a value an evaluator reads is skipped by name rather than read as a nought, while a zone count of nought and a crossing count of nought are scored; the pre-pass makes no request and one median reaches every verdict; every pin agrees; each candidate's per-night counts over the stored nights are re-derived from a copy of the store and recorded in the entry, the fixture's own night standing as the derived expectation, since the store's nights are not committed and cannot be a fixture file; the crossing counts at 0.25 and at 0.5 are brought to the operator before the registration command is written; the command registers the three at one instant or none of them; and the looks' power is re-derived on the pinned calibration and stated in section 17.

### 10.4 The trend rule versioned
The trend rule becomes the fifth ladder rule the rule version scorer replays, with versions A, B and C opened by `RUNBOOK.md` commands, the cap at once raised to 18, and a version judged on its difference from the live rule (see: The trend rule is a fifth ladder rule a version replays, and none of its three versions is live) (see: A trend version is judged by the candidates' test on its difference from the live rule).

**Done when** the live parameters replay every stored plan in the fixture, each version produces a plan worked by hand over constructed input, a range name closing below both averages and a label that flips for one night among them; the version populations are recounted by cause as a derived expectation; the record of the setups A removes and B keeps is reported under the live rule and tested nowhere; a paired difference is worked by hand; section 17 carries the cap of 18 with its constant; the run page's rule versions region states each trend version's labels and how often a label returned (owes: The trend confirmation's nights settled from flip-backs); and the fullest register's projected night is stated from section 17's night row.

### 10.5 Phase 10 report
`tools/verify-phase` reaches every claim this phase added, and the entry states what is owed to evidence the nights produce, with the calendar of every candidate's and version's looks.

**Done when** the phase report is green with no claim unexamined and the entry names every operating row this phase opened with its trigger.

## Phase 11: a report written when it is asked for, and four parts it gains

**Visible output at 11.1.** A press asking for a report in the off-peak hours starts that report's pass at once, and the queue page shows it being written.

**Opened on the operator's rulings of 2026-09-23, after phase 10's sign-off.** Reading the live screens that evening, the operator asked for four things about the queue, in their words: "a selected name should have a report pre-generated after the run", for "just the top 1st name for now"; "I dont want nightly script to do it. I want the queue to be processed as soon as i queue them", and, offered a watcher that finds a request, a task that looks every five minutes and the page starting it on the press, they chose the page; "off peak hours it is for everything"; and "i want the user to know fully when this report will generate on the queue page". Later the same evening they added four report parts to this phase, written first as 10.6 to 10.10 and relabelled by the operator as phase 11's: each large move beside its group's median move, a peers table by price alone, the earnings reaction record and the dividend lines as the provider files them. A report on DUK dated 2026-09-18, which the operator keeps beside the repository, is the picture they come from, and the four are the part of it the store can hold.

**What this phase is for.** Phase 9 built the queue and nothing that drains it on its own. A press writes a request, and the one thing that writes the report is the drain typed by hand, so a request waits for someone to type it and no screen says whether anything will. The operator's first request, for DGX at 19:01 UTC on 2026-09-23, waited until a drain run by hand took it at 19:09. This phase makes a press start its pass, keeps every paid pass in the off-peak hours, states on the queue page when each request will be written, has the night ask for a report on the first name of its list, and adds the four parts to a name's report, each computed from what the night already fetches or stores and none adding a request.

**The four parts are display only.** None reaches a reason, a gate, a plan, a listing, the shadow column or the candidate register (see: Candidate conditions are registered before they are scored, and a candidate's picks are shown on the Run page while its outcomes wait for a look). Registering any of them as a candidate is a separate ruling this phase does not make, no claim checker rule changes, and the report keeps its eleven sections with nothing renumbered.

**What reading main found, and how the plan adapts.** Four findings, read off main at 7c7f136 rather than off a run:
- The fixture holds bars for four members, AAPL, MSFT and KEYS in Technology and NFLX in Communication Services, each in an industry of its own. No industry reaches the group floor, so the fixture reaches the sector fallback, where each Technology name's group is the other two and NFLX's holds nobody, and never the industry path. The group rule's done conditions use constructed input for the industry path, with a measurement over a copy of the operator's store beside it.
- The universe table's rows come from one read, `ReadApi.UniverseAsync`, which carries each member's stored close, trend state, nearest bands, typical move, sector and industry. The peers table reads the same call filtered to the name's group, so the two cannot disagree about a member.
- The earnings rule reads a print's timing inside `LadderSeries.EarningsRuleFor`, which takes the session a print moved: the report's own day for one before the open or one whose timing the provider left unstated, and the next session for one after the close. It is not a function of its own, and making it one would edit `LadderSeries.cs`, which the ladder rules' code version and every evaluator's version both pin, so every open window and the three candidates registered that day would move. The reaction record calls `EarningsRuleFor` one print at a time instead, which takes the same session by the same code and edits no pinned source. The stored timing's words are read back through the calendar fetcher, which writes them.
- The indicator engine is one of the sources every evaluator's version pins, so the two readings the peers table needs cannot be written there without moving all five versions. The operator ruled on 2026-09-23 that the move annotator writes them in a table of its own, which no version pins.

**Each part's schema, catalogue row and matrix cells land with its code.** `SCHEMA.md` declares a column or a table at the checkpoint that migrates it, as 9.2 wrote the request store with its migration, and the catalogue rows and the matrix change with the declarations `component-access` reconciles them against, which it reads off the code. Section 17's group floor of 5 lands at 11.5 and the return window of 60 at 11.6, each with its constant.

**The architecture's rows for the four parts are written by a document pass at 11.0, before 11.1 builds anything.** Sections 4, 15.9 and 15.12 and the exporter's rows gain the parts and where each sits, each with its key under it (see: The key under each figure is dated by the night whose figures it explains, written for every name each night, and drawn only beside that night's figures). The pass places every claim they add at the checkpoint that ends it, out of scope until then, and predicts the claims and PASS pair after 11.9 with a named cause at each end of its range.

**The claims phase 11 predicts.** 413 claims and 409 PASS after the document pass, with 4 out of scope, 0 unexamined and 0 fail: the pass adds the name screen's four rows, each out of scope until the checkpoint that draws it, 1 at 11.5, 1 at 11.6, 1 at 11.7 and 1 at 11.8. After 11.9 the pair is 421 and 421 with 0 out of scope, from eight more claims, each named at the checkpoint that adds it and passing there: the queue screen's row stating when each request will be written at 11.2; tonight's row stating the report's state at 11.3; section 14's step writing the night's request and section 17's count of one at 11.4; section 17's group floor at 11.5; section 17's return window and section 16's store for the peers' readings at 11.6; and section 16's store for the reaction record at 11.7. 11.1 and 11.8 add none, 11.1 changing what the queue screen's paragraph and the read API's catalogue row say and 11.8 adding a part to a payload section 16 already carries. The range is 419 to 425. Its low end is 419 if the night's request is written inside the close's step rather than as a step of its own and tonight's report state is a part of the research row that already claims it rather than a row. Its high end is 425 if the queue screen's row is read as its three parts, being when a pass starts, when it ends and the zone both are stated in, and the peers' readings and the reaction record each arrive with a row of their own in section 19.1's fixture table.

**The decision a press supersedes is superseded at 11.1 and not here.** "A request the page writes and the worker drains is what starts a pass, and the read surface writes the ask and never the research" describes what the shipped source does until 11.1 changes it, and 9.2's done condition asserts over the shipped source that a press starts no process. A decision moved to Previously decided while its code still runs describes a mechanism nothing has built, as 9.0 said of the queue and 10.0 of the order. So 11.1 writes the replacement, moves the old entry with its reasoning, repoints its citations and replaces the assertion that a press starts no process with the one that it starts one drain, in the same commit.

**What a press starts is the worker's own drain, and the request row stays the record.** The read surface starts the worker's drain as a process of its own and holds no reference to the worker, so `api-isolation` stands. Every reason the superseded decision gave for the queue survives the change, because the request is still written first: the queue screen lists it, a request nobody has claimed can still be taken out, and a press that started nothing is still told apart from one whose pass died. The drain runs from a copy of the worker's build output made for it, so a drain still writing when the night's build replaces that output holds none of it and cannot stop the night. A drain started while another runs takes only what that one has not claimed, since a claim is one write only one drain can make.

**Every pass is paid at the off-peak rate.** Today only the theme research runner refuses to start at peak, and a name's pass run by the drain at peak is billed at double. From 11.1 a drain that starts inside a peak window waits until the pricing's first off-peak instant before its first pass, and one that reaches a peak window between passes waits there (see: Queued work runs off-peak, and every schedule is written in UTC). The windows are the pricing's own, 01:00 to 04:00 and 06:00 to 10:00 UTC on weekdays, which are 21:00 to 24:00 and 02:00 to 06:00 in New York until 2026-11-01 and an hour earlier after it.

**When each request will be written is stated, not left to a reader.** The queue page states for each outstanding request the instant its pass will start: now, the end of the peak window it was asked in, or after the requests ahead of it on an estimate. For a request being written it states when its pass started and when it is expected to end, and for a settled one when it was written or refused. Times are New York's, with the offset named and UTC beside them, read through the clock's own zone, so the change on 2026-11-01 moves what the page says and nothing else. The estimate is the median duration of the passes the store holds, stated with how many it is over, and a store holding none says it cannot estimate. The operator's store held three on 2026-09-23, at 4, 26 and 29 minutes.

**The night asks for its first name, and its arithmetic still calls no model.** After its close the night writes one request for the first name drawn on its list, marked as asked by the night, and starts the drain as a press does. What the night itself does is write one row; the pass is the drain's own run, outside the night, with its calls on its own rows. It is still one paid pass a night set off by the nightly job, and the operator ruled on 2026-09-23, in their words "Yes the nightly run will generate paid research for top 1 name", that the hard rule's carve-outs name it, a third beside the new member's backfill and the corporate action refetch, as one name a night that does not grow with the index. 11.4 writes it into the hard rule with the code that makes the request.

### 11.0 Planning
Writes this section, the decisions the phase rests on that describe no running code, and the record of the two report additions an SNPS report owed. Builds none of it. A document pass at 11.0 writes the architecture's rows for the four parts.

**Done when** every decision the checkpoints below cite resolves, no done condition below waits on a night the calendar produces, and each checkpoint below names the parts of the corpus it writes.

### 11.1 A press starts the pass
A press writes its request and starts the worker's drain from a copy of its build output; the drain waits out a peak window before any pass; and the decision the queue rests on is superseded with its citations repointed. Section 15.15, the read surface's row in the component catalogue and `RUNBOOK.md`'s section on draining the queue say what a press does.

**Done when** a press over either hosted route writes one request and asks one launcher to start one drain, read over a launcher the test supplies, and the shipped source starts that process in one place; a drain started at an instant inside each peak window waits until the pricing's first off-peak instant and one started outside begins at once, over constructed instants on both sides of every window's edges; the process is started from a copy and never from the worker's own build output, read off the path it is given; a second drain started while one runs claims no request the first holds; the superseded decision moves with its reasoning and every citation of it is repointed; and 9.2's assertion that a press starts no process is replaced by the one that it starts one drain.

The correction of 2026-09-24 bounds every start by the longest pass the store holds that ran to its end, and the run page counts each paid call answered inside a peak window (see: A pass starts only where the longest pass the store holds would end before a peak window opens) (owes: A paid call answered inside a peak window).

### 11.2 When each request will be written
The queue page states, for every request it lists, when its pass will start or started and when it is expected to end or ended, in New York's time with UTC beside it. Drains run one at a time over a store, one started while another runs waiting for it to end, so the order the page states is the order passes run in.

**Done when** over constructed stores and instants the page states now for an outstanding request with nothing ahead of it off-peak, the end of the peak window for one asked inside it, and the estimated start after the requests ahead of it, each read back off the page against a computation of the test's own; the estimate is the median over the passes the store holds and names how many it is over, and a store holding none says it cannot estimate; the times are New York's with the offset named, on both sides of 2026-11-01's change, with UTC beside them; and section 15.15 states it.

The queue page's count of the passes its estimate is taken over is where the start bound is reviewed (owes: The start bound reviewed at twenty passes).

### 11.3 The report's state where a name is read
Tonight's rows and the selected name's region state what the queue holds for the name: written with its date, being written since an instant, queued with the instant it will start, or not written with the control asking for one.

**Done when** each state is read back off both surfaces against the store in both directions, over a store holding a request in each state, and section 15.7's research row states it.

### 11.4 The night asks for its first name
After its close the night writes one request for the first name drawn on its list, asked by the night, and starts the drain as a press does. The request table's `asked_from` admits the night through a migration, the queue page names who asked, and the count, one, is a constant section 17 pins.

**Done when** over the fixture's night the request is written for the list's first name and no other; a name with a request outstanding or being written gets none and the night's run log row says so; the night's own steps record no model call and none of the pass's network requests, which sit on the drain's own run; the migration applies and `schema-columns` reads the widened check; `SCHEMA.md` declares the night a writer of the request table's insert, split from the read surface's by operation; sections 14 and 17 state it; and the hard rule states it as the operator ruled before this checkpoint.

### 11.5 A move beside its group
The move annotator reads the membership and writes each large move's group median on the move row it already owns, with the group it used and how many members it counted (see: A large move is shown beside its group's median move over the same sessions). The group is the one rule declared once for every surface (see: A name's group is its industry where at least five other members share it on the session, and its sector otherwise, and every surface that uses it says which and how many). Section 2's move table on a name page draws the median beside each move and names the group.

**Done when** the median is worked by hand over constructed input, an even member count, a member missing the span's first close and an industry below the floor that falls back to its sector among it; the name is never in its own group; the fixture's sector fallback is derived by hand, each Technology name's group being the other two and NFLX's holding nobody and saying so; section 17 carries the floor of 5 with its constant; `SCHEMA.md`, the catalogue and the matrix state the move table's new columns and the membership read; and DUK's stored moves are measured over a copy of the operator's store for 2026-09-18, opened immutable, and recorded in the entry with each move's group, count and median.

### 11.6 The peers table
The move annotator writes two readings for every member, each with its bar count, in a table of its own: the distance below the stored year's high and the 60-session return. Section 2 on a name page draws the peers table under the move table, the name's own row marked and then at most ten members of its group by price alone, those sharing its industry first and then by how closely each one's daily moves followed the name's, carrying that likeness, the close, the distance below the high, the return, the trend state and the distance row mark (see: Peers are shown by price alone, ten at most with the name's industry first and then the members whose daily moves followed it most closely).

**Done when** both readings are worked by hand over the fixture and at their edges, a name holding fewer bars than the return's window showing it as not available with its bar count rather than over a shorter window; the page computes nothing (see: A screen reads and renders, and computes only the plan in the operator's money and a pick's open trades in its sector); the distance to the nearest band is stated in typical days (see: Distances are stated as typical days' moves); the table lists every member of the group and ranks none; section 17 carries the window of 60 with its constant; `SCHEMA.md`, the catalogue and the matrix state the new table and its writer; and DUK's group from the same store copy is recorded in the entry with every member's figures.

### 11.7 The earnings reaction record
A table the move annotator owns, one row per ticker and print over the calendar's year behind: report date, timing, reaction session, estimate, actual, the provider's surprise and the reaction session's move. The reaction session is the one `EarningsRuleFor` takes for the print, called one print at a time (see: Each print's reaction is read from the nightly calendar and the stored bars, and the earnings drift is the one rule that reads it). Section 4 on a name page draws the record beside the earnings-trade setups.

**Done when** every column is derived by hand from the captured calendar and bars for the fixture's names, a print with no filed estimate, each of the three timings and a print whose reaction session the stored bars do not reach among them, that last dropped and counted rather than guessed; a print with no filed estimate states its actual and says none was filed, and is never counted as a meet; the table carries one row for every print the calendar's year behind holds for a name whose bars reach it; retention drops a print with its bars (see: The bar store holds one year for every night's work, and the history pulled beside it is read by measurements alone); every rule version's pin and every evaluator's stands as it stood; a test asserts that no reason, gate, plan or candidate evaluator reads the table; and `SCHEMA.md`, the catalogue and the matrix state the table with its grain, its key, its writer as its deleter and its retention (see: Every computed table's writer is its own deleter).

### 11.8 The dividend lines
The fundamentals fetcher parses the provider's SplitsDividends part into a dividend part on the newest filing's row, and section 6 draws it: the forward annual rate, the forward yield as the provider states it, the payout ratio, the ex-dividend date and the pay date (see: The numbers section draws the dividend the provider files from the newest filing alone, and nothing for a company paying none).

**Done when** the four captured payloads, AAPL, KEYS, MSFT and NFLX, parse with every value matching its file; a payer and a non-payer each draw as stated, the non-payer drawing no dividend part since the operator's ruling of 2026-09-24 and a row holding no dividend part saying it is absent and why; the source column names the provider for the part; and `SCHEMA.md`'s fundamentals payload note gains the dividend part.

### 11.9 Phase 11 report
`tools/verify-phase` reaches every claim 11.1 to 11.8 added, and the pair the document pass predicted is checked against the actual, with every claim that moved named. The facts file gains each move's group median with its group and count, each print's reaction and the dividend, so prose may quote them (see: Every number in written prose must exist in the facts file) (see: A name's facts file is assembled again for its night when an open fetches its fundamentals). They were planned at 11.5, 11.7 and 11.8 and moved here when 11.5 found that the fixture's model answers are recorded against prompts carrying the facts file, so a figure added to it refuses every recording at once: the local recordings are made again on the local model, and the paid ones on a spend the operator agrees before they are made.

On the operator's addendum of 2026-09-23 it also replaces section 11's thresholds paragraph with a target, a rule and an exclusion: each reason's median nightly share of the index at or below 2% and the share firing any reason at or below 6%, both proposed; a night on which a reason whose own median nightly share is below a quarter fires for more than a quarter of the index an event session, counted and left out of the sample; and each threshold moved once after sixty ordinary nights (see: The swing filter's shape is calibrated over its ordinary nights, a night one cause pushes past a quarter and twice its usual share is left out, and each band spans a third to three times what the ruled filter passes). Section 17 gains the two targets and the event share, the run page gains a region stating each reason's nightly share against its target with the event sessions marked, the obligation the calibration answers is told both, and the eight nights from 2026-09-09 to 2026-09-18 are measured over the rows written after the 5.4 corrections and recorded.

**Done when** the phase report is green with no claim unexamined and the predicted pair is checked against the actual; the facts file carries the medians, the reactions and the dividend, asserted over the fixture; every fixture recording the facts file reaches is made again, with the suite green over them; the run page states for the night it is read for each reason's share of the index, its median over the ordinary nights and its target, the same three for the share firing any reason, the ordinary nights counted against sixty and every event session with the reason that made it one, each read back off the page against a computation of the test's own over constructed nights, among them a reason above a quarter on every night marking none, a usually quiet reason above a quarter on one night marking it and that night left out of every median, and a row written before the 5.4 corrections counted for neither reason they changed; and section 17 carries the two targets and the event share, each held by its constant.
