**Done when** the counterfactual recomputation is unreachable from the edge half, asserted by behaviour and by a scan of the source; a candidate below the block floor shows no verdict; a constructed near-miss population with known outcomes gives the attribution its arithmetic predicts; the twenty-session outcome is worked by hand over constructed bars on both sides of its cap; every figure in the half is read back off the rendered page; and the entry states the pin list read and that its tree touched none of it.

### 12.8 The worked example
Section 11 gains a worked example in five figures drawn in the document's own colour variables, support green and resistance orange alone, each with a key in plain words saying how to read it and what to take from it: the funnel of one night; one name through every gate, its chart, the support band, the recent high, the pullback in typical days, the trigger session and the plan with its reward to risk worked out; the same setup resolving; the two clocks on a timeline; and near-miss attribution. The first three are computed from the committed fixture by the code, the fixture date in each key, and where no fixture name passes every gate the second draws the one that passes the most and names the gate that stopped it; the third replays an earlier fixture session so a setup can resolve. The last two are illustrative, labelled so, and none of their figures appears in a limit, a decision or a claim. Section 1's purpose sentence gains the operator's goal and says the list is chosen by facts about the chart and the plan while its chance of success is measured by the loop over time rather than asserted, and section 3 gains the words the phase uses.

**Done when** the first three figures are regenerated from the fixture by the code and their numbers match the fixture's expectations; every figure is placed and fits its column; the keys name the fixture date or say illustrative; section 3 carries every word the phase uses in one sentence each; and the entry states the pin list read and that its tree touched none of it.

### 12.9 Phase 12 report
`tools/verify-phase` reaches every claim this phase added, and the pair predicted above is checked against the actual with every claim that moved named. Every evaluation against the operator's store is run again over the nights since, with what moved stated. No sentence of the architecture describes a list chosen by any reason firing as the live one.

**Done when** the phase report is green with no claim unexamined and the pair is checked against the actual, and the entry names every operating row this phase opened with its trigger.

## Phase 13: setup families, built and shown first and each swept and frozen after

**Visible output at 13.1.** Tonight's page draws the night's list as a card for the pullback family in place of the one list: its rule in a sentence, the day its rule went live, each stock it lists with the buy, the stop and the target of its trade and why it is listed tonight, and a note for a stock it passed that a trade still open holds back.

**Opened before phase 12's sign-off, on the operator's ruling of 2026-10-01** (see: Setup families are built as phase 13 beside phase 12's sign-off, which runs in a fresh session over what merged on 2026-10-01). That sign-off stays owed on phase 12 as a whole, runs in a fresh session beside this phase, and nothing in this phase discharges it. The ruling moves the order of the two and nothing else.

**What this phase is for.** One rule, the pullback, chose tonight's list. The operator ruled four setup families, each a rule of its own with its own picks, variants, record and checkpoints: pullbacks, today's swing filter as it stands; breakouts, a close above the year's high on heavy volume after the daily ranges tightened, sold on a trailing stop; earnings drift, a report that beat its estimate with a strong reaction, bought within days of it; and sector leaders, the strongest stocks of the strongest sectors at a pullback's buy point. Each new family goes live on provisional settings taken from published evidence, marked as not yet frozen, and is then swept over the stored history, its report proposing the starting point that replaces them at its freeze on the operator's go (see: A family lists on provisional settings until its freeze, and nothing before the freeze counts toward a checkpoint). The families share the night's machinery, one market check, one open trade rule and one page, and the page's list is drawn from all of them by one rule: five a family, a stock once, and none while a trade for it is still open (see: Tonight's page is drawn from setup families, each a rule of its own listing at most five a night) (see: A stock holds one trade across every swing family, and one qualifying under two is listed once under the first in the page's order) (see: The market check closes every swing family's list together, and the sector heavyweights read none).

**What reading main and the store found, and how the plan adapts.** The ruling was read against the code at c844843 and the operator's store at user_version 52, opened read-only, and ten things came back; the counts are a planning estimate by independent arithmetic over the pulled history, 1,950 nights from 2019-01-02 to 2026-09-30, the market check open on 1,416 of them at the open version's floor. Each was put to the operator with a recommendation on 2026-10-01 and the build goes on the recommendations at their word to start building.
- The store keeps 252 sessions a name, tonight among them, so a close is read against the highest high of the 251 sessions before it and no more, and the breakout's rule says 251.
- "Ranges tightened" names no number. The mean daily range, as a share of the close, of the 20 sessions before tonight no wider than that of the 20 before them lists a breakout on 53 per cent of open nights, 1.6 a night; at 0.85 of it, on 31 per cent. The rule takes no wider, and the breakouts' sweep settles it.
- Sector leaders as written have no buy point: about 33 stocks qualify every night and the same ones the next, and 10 to 23 of them a night held a plan passing the trade gate over the 16 stored nights from 2026-09-09. So "entry, stop and target as the pullback's" is read to include the pullback's buy point, its setup, trigger and trade gates, with sector leadership in place of its trend and strength gate; over the 8 stored gate nights that lists 0, 0, 1, 1, 3, 5, 2 and 2, all but 2 of the 14 stocks the pullback does not list.
- A breakout names no target, so it has no win, no loss and no break-even. Its record is its average result in multiples of its risk, the figure the sweep counts every trade in, and its checkpoint test is fixed in its sweep's report before its freeze; until then its card draws each trade's result and no share.
- The ruling's order builds the pages sixth, so nothing would show until then. Each family's card lands with the checkpoint that builds the family, and the pages' checkpoint carries the market line, the shared list of stocks close to a buy point, Past picks' filter and label and the run page.
- The mockup's market line names the index against its 50 and 200-day averages, and the night holds no index series. The line draws breadth and whether the lists are open; the index joins it only if the pullback's freeze adds a switch that reads one.
- A family's checkpoint record counts every trade its own rule would have made, the ones the page gave to another family among them; the page's one trade a stock is the lister's. Otherwise one family's change would move every family's record.
- The correction for luck is a family's own: at most eight rules tested together a family, where eight stood for the whole register. It binds at the freezes, 13.9, and no checkpoint before it registers anything.
- The earnings calendar's one request already carries each print's estimate, actual and surprise for the year behind, and the move annotator already stores each print's reaction session and move, so the earnings drift adds no request and no fetch. A print before the open carried its actual on the night of 2026-09-30 and one after the close did not, its reaction falling on the next session; the five-session window covers an actual posted late.
- Of 16,241 beats in the pulled prints, 4,702 had a reaction up a typical move on 1.5 times volume. Inside its five-session window the drift qualifies 8.4 stocks on an open night and more than five on 37 per cent of them, so its order decides its card in a reporting season.

**Where the code lives.** No source any pin holds is edited before the freezes: the twelve the ladder rules' code version pins, the sources every candidate evaluator's version pins, and the sixteen the swing filter's code version pins. The pullback family reads the rows the swing filter already stores, each new family's rule lives in files of its own, and the page's list is drawn by a component of its own after the filter. The pullback's freeze is the one pin move, as the queue ruling of 2026-09-30 planned it, and it also carries the three new families' evaluators into the register's list, each pinning its own files, because that list is a source every evaluator pins: done once there, a later family's freeze writes register rows and moves no other family's version. Every entry states the pin lists read and that its tree touched none.

**One pull request, and one run of the gates.** On the operator's word of 2026-10-01, "Build the whole thing and then create one big PR on remote", the checkpoints 13.0 to 13.5 are built on one branch and reach main as one pull request, each as its own commit with its own entry written before the run, and both gates are run once over the tree carrying every one of those entries, each entry's figures filled from that run. Each checkpoint's mutations are made against that tree in an isolated worktree and reverted.

**Two things are the operator's.** Each family's freeze, which registers its rule and its variants at one instant and cannot be undone, and the go on each sweep's proposal before it. Nothing is frozen or registered by any checkpoint from 13.0 to 13.8.

**The claims phase 13 predicts.** 789 claims and 789 PASS before it. After 13.5 the pair is 853 and 853, within 840 to 870: 25 at 13.1, being the lister's catalogue and matrix rows, its two stores, section 17's five a family, section 18's three rows and the card as seventeen parts, three of them drawn at 13.2 once a second family is on the page; about 10 at 13.2, a store, a component's two rows, the breakout's settings and its two failure rows; about 7 at 13.3 and 5 at 13.4, each family's settings and failure rows; and about 17 at 13.5, the market line, the shared list's setup label, Past picks' filter and label, the name page's why and the run page's families. The planning pass adds none: section 20's row is read by the check that counts the phases and is no claim.

### 13.0 Planning
Writes this section, the decisions the phase rests on that describe no running code, its order beside phase 12's sign-off and the four the framework is built to, with the two earlier decisions they narrow, and section 20's row for phase 13. Builds none of it.

**Done when** every decision the checkpoints below cite resolves, no done condition below waits on the calendar, each checkpoint below names the parts of the corpus it writes, and section 20 carries one row for every phase this plan holds.

### 13.1 The family framework, with the pullback as its first family
The family lister draws the page's list each night, in the swing filter's step and after the filter has stored its rows: it reads each family's passing names in that family's own order, the pullback's being the names the filter passed, improving businesses first, and every trade a list made on an earlier night with what became of it, and stores the list, five a family in the page's order, a stock once, and none whose trade from any family is still open, each row held back saying why, recording the session as one the families drew. Every reader of tonight's list reads that stored list on a night the families drew and the list as it was on a night before them: the page, the overnight queue, the night's requests, the news labeller, the night's count, the facts retention and Past picks. The night asks for a report on the first six names its page draws, where it asked for the first alone. Tonight's page draws a card a family in place of the one list on such a night, the pullback's card with its rule in a sentence, the day its rule went live or the words saying it is provisional, its picks each with its trade and why it is listed, and the notes on what it passed and the page holds back. Writes the family lister's catalogue and matrix rows, section 14's step, section 15.7's card, the two stores in section 16 and `SCHEMA.md`, section 17's five a family and six reports, section 18's three rows, and the decision asking for six reports, the one asking for the first name alone moved to Previously decided with every citation of it repointed, the first hard rule's among them.

**Done when** the list is worked by hand over three constructed families, a family passing exactly five among them, a stock two pass, a stock past an earlier family's five and a stock holding an open trade; the lister over a constructed store lists by the pullback's own order, holds back a stock whose earlier trade is undecided or whose outcome row is missing and frees one stopped out the session before, replaces its own night where it is run again, reads the page's own earlier trades on the next night with a stop's night still holding the stock, and draws nothing on a night the filter stored no result; a night is read as listed by the page where the families drew it, by the filter's passing names on a night before them and by the reasons before that, off the read surface's own rows; the night asks for the first six down a constructed page of seven and for none where every stock is held back; the card is read back off the rendered page against the store in both directions, its trade, its place between the stop and the target worked by hand, its words against the figures its gates stored, its notes, a card that lists nothing saying why on a closed market and on a night no stock passed, and a night before the families drawn as it was; migration 53 applies and `schema-columns` reads both stores; and the entry states the three pin lists read and that its tree touched none.

### 13.2 Breakouts
The breakout family evaluates every member every night: the market check; a close above the highest high of the 251 sessions before it; volume at least 1.5 times its 50-session average; and the mean daily range of the 20 sessions before tonight, as a share of the close, no wider than that of the 20 before them. Its trade is bought at the close, its stop 2 typical moves beneath and trailing 2 typical moves under the highest close since the buy, never lowered, with no target and 63 sessions at most; its order is the volume against its average, largest first. Every member's answer is stored with the values that decided it, the family's trades are scored by the forward return filler under a horizon of their own, sold at the first close under the trail or at the cap, and its card draws its picks with the stop, no target and why each is listed. With a second family on the page, a stock two families pass is drawn once with the other's label and named in the later card's note. Writes the family's rule and its provisional settings into section 11, its component's catalogue and matrix rows, its store in section 16 and `SCHEMA.md`, section 17's rows for its settings, each marked provisional, section 18's rows for a member holding too few sessions and a trade with no typical move, and the decision the family rests on.

**Done when** each gate is worked by hand over constructed bars on both sides of its threshold and exactly at it, a member one session short of the year reading not available with its count; the trailing exit is worked by hand over constructed closes, the stop raised with a new high close and never lowered, a close under it selling and the cap ending a trade still open, with the result in multiples of the risk; the order is worked by hand; a stock two families pass is read back off the page once, with the label and the note; the card's rows are read back off the rendered page against the store; and the entry states the pin lists read and that its tree touched none.

### 13.3 Earnings drift
The earnings drift family evaluates every member every night over the reactions the move annotator stores: the market check; a print whose reaction session is tonight or one of the four sessions before; the provider's surprise above zero; the reaction session's close up at least one typical move of the session before it; that session's volume at least 1.5 times its 50-session average; and tonight's close above the reaction session's low. Its trade is bought at the close, stopped at the reaction session's low, and its target is the nearer of the lowest band two typical moves or more above the close and 2.5 times the risk, with 60 sessions at most; its order is the surprise, largest first. Writes the family's rule and settings into section 11, section 17's rows for its settings, each marked provisional, section 18's rows for a print whose actual the calendar does not yet carry and a reaction session the stored bars do not reach, and the decision the family rests on, the decision that a print's reaction reaches no gate superseded by one naming the earnings drift as the one rule that reads it, with every citation repointed.

**Done when** each gate is worked by hand over constructed prints and bars on both sides of its threshold and exactly at it, a print after the close taking the next session and one with no actual passing nothing; the window is worked at its first and last session and one past it; the target is worked by hand where the band is nearer and where 2.5 times the risk is; the cap of 60 is scored by hand; the card's rows are read back off the rendered page against the store; and the entry states the pin lists read and that its tree touched none.

### 13.4 Sector leaders
The sector leader family evaluates every member every night: the market check; its sector among the top 3 of the sectors ranked by the median 126-session return of their members; its own return in the top quarter of its sector; and the pullback's setup, trigger and trade gates as the swing filter stored them that night, on the pullback's plan. Its order is the sector's rank and then the stock's return inside it. Writes the family's rule and settings into section 11, section 17's rows for its settings, each marked provisional, section 18's rows for a member the membership names no sector for and a sector too small to rank, and the decision the family rests on.

**Done when** the sectors' ranking is worked by hand over constructed members, a tie and a sector one member short of the floor among them; the top quarter is worked at its boundary; a leader the swing filter's stored setup, trigger or trade gate fails is not passed and one failing its trend gate alone is; a stock the pullback and this family both pass is listed under the pullback with this family's label; the card's rows are read back off the rendered page against the store; and the entry states the pin lists read and that its tree touched none.

### 13.5 The pages
Tonight opens on a market line, the night's breadth against its floor and whether the lists are open, the buy points across the setups, the stocks close to a buy point and the open trades, linking to Past picks. "Close to a buy point" is one list across every family with a setup label a row and the one gate each missed. Past picks gains a setup filter and a label a trade, and its trailing trades are drawn with no target and their result in multiples of the risk. A name's page says under which setup the page listed it. The run page draws each family: live or provisional, the day its rule went live, its variants, its trades so far and, for a registered family, its clock against its checkpoints. Writes section 15.7, 15.9, 15.10 and 15.17.

**Done when** every figure on the market line is read back off the rendered page against the store over a constructed night, an open and a closed market among them; the shared list's rows are read back with their labels in both directions; Past picks' filter keeps exactly the family's trades and its counts follow; the run page's family region is read back for a registered family and a provisional one; and the entry states the pin lists read and that its tree touched none.

### 13.6 The breakouts' sweep
The sweep's machinery replays the breakout family over the stored history, across its settings, on the edge over the same plan entered on every member, with one open trade a stock, and writes a report in a run folder of its own proposing a starting point and variants, with the test its checkpoints will read fixed in the report, or setting the family aside with its figures where nothing is steady.

**Done when** the replay's readings on a sampled session equal the night's own for that session; the trailing exit's benchmark is worked by hand over constructed members; the report's figures are read back against a constructed history with a known answer; and the command that runs it is written in `RUNBOOK.md`.

### 13.7 The earnings drift's sweep
As 13.6, over the earnings drift family and the pulled surprises.

**Done when** the replay's readings on a sampled session equal the night's own; a print the pulled surprises hold and the calendar's year does not is read from the pull; and the report's figures are read back against a constructed history with a known answer.

### 13.8 The sector leaders' sweep
As 13.6, over the sector leader family, stating how many of the history's names carry a sector and how many do not.

**Done when** the replay's readings on a sampled session equal the night's own; a name the membership names no sector for is left out and counted; and the report's figures are read back against a constructed history with a known answer.

### 13.9 The freezes
One command a family writes its registrations at one instant, the family's live rule at the starting point its sweep proposed and the operator approved, and its variants, or none; the pullback's, taken first, carries the base as its frozen starting point, today's swing filter with its reward-to-risk floor raised to 2 and every other setting as it stands (see: The pullback freezes at the base with its reward-to-risk floor raised to 2, and the ideas' run waits until phase 13 is finished), opened as the next filter version with every standing swing family candidate registered again beside it (see: The pullback's freeze opens its base as the next filter version and registers the swing family again at one instant), the open trade rule inside the rule, the fundamentals candidate held for it, the sector leaders registered beside them as a variant of the base, sector leadership in place of its trend and strength gate, with their card leaving Tonight (see: The sector leaders are a variant of the pullback's starting point and not a family of their own), and the breakout's and the drift's evaluators joining the register's list. The breakouts and the earnings drift freeze at the starting points the operator approved on 2026-10-02, each proposal first shown to sit where its one-step neighbours also meet the floors, each with those neighbours as its variants and the breakout's provisional setting and the drift's rule with its stop no closer than one typical move beside them (see: The new families freeze at their sweeps' proposals, the breakout's provisional setting and the drift's wider stop registered beside them as variants). Each new family's rule reads its settings, so a variant is the same rule at other settings; the family evaluator evaluates every family candidate standing when the night started over every member at that candidate's settings and stores each verdict on the member's row; each registered rule keeps its own list, five a night in its own order and one open trade a stock, and the night stores each trade it keeps and, once the trade ends, its result and the benchmark of the same plan entered on every member that night; and the run page reads each registered family's record, its trades' edge over blocks of 63 sessions against the test its sweep's report fixed (see: A registered family rule is evaluated every night at its own settings and keeps its own list, its trades stored with their benchmark when they end), its correction counting the family's own rules (see: Each setup family's correction for luck counts its own rules alone, at most nine a family). The four freezes land in one pull request with one remedy, on the operator's ruling of 2026-10-02. A family set aside registers nothing and its card says so. `RUNBOOK.md` states that each command runs after its merge and before the night.

**Done when** each command writes its rows at one instant or none, refusing all of them when one is refused; every evaluator's version is the pin of its own sources; the leaders' variant is worked by hand, firing on a member inside the top sectors and its share whatever its trend gate says and not on one outside them; the sector leaders' card is gone from Tonight and a pick an earlier night listed under them is still named as one; each new family's rule is worked by hand at a variant's settings on both sides of every setting the variant moves; a family candidate's verdict is stored on every member's row and a moved evaluator fails the stage; a registered rule's list is worked by hand over constructed verdicts, five a night in its order with one open trade a stock and a stock freed the night after its trade ends; a kept trade's result and benchmark are worked by hand over constructed bars for a trailing and a fixed exit; a registered rule's record is read back off the run page over constructed trades, its blocks and its test; a registered family's card reads the day its rule went live off the register; the family's correction divides by its own rules and no other family's; and each entry names every pinned file it edited and the remedy it issued through the remedy script.

### 13.10 Phase 13 report
`tools/verify-phase` reaches every claim this phase added, and the pair predicted above is checked against the actual with every claim that moved named.

**Done when** the phase report is green with no claim unexamined and the pair is checked against the actual.

## Phase 14: sector heavyweights, context checks on the setups, and the S&P 1500 data check

**Visible output at 14.1.** The run page states, beside each family rule whose code the change of 2026-10-03 moved, whether its record carries on from its first registration or restarts, and why.

**What this phase is for.** The operator's brief of 2026-10-03 asked for three things, each evaluated and its data checked before anything is built. A fourth family, the sector heavyweights: the largest companies of each sector held while they lead it, rotated monthly, with a scoring, a record and a card of their own. Context checks on the frozen setups, the drift's revenue quality, news on the pullback, analyst revisions and orderings, each tested over the history before any joins a rule. And a data check on whether a later phase can widen the universe to the S&P 1500, reported and nothing built. Beside them the operator set a standing decision: no model's judgment chooses, filters or orders a pick, and models write the reports (see: Rules choose the stocks, and the AI writes the reports).

**What the data checks found, read-only on 2026-10-03.** The store was opened read-only; the provider's answers cost 1,234 of the day's 100,000 weighted calls, and the captures behind each finding are in the fixture's manifest under the prefix `phase14-`.
- A company's value as it stood is computable. The provider files every company's quarterly share count for current and departed members, AAPL 34 quarters since 2018 and ATVI 22 to its delisting on 2023-10-13, and every quarterly balance sheet carries the date it was filed, AAPL's 28 to 36 days after its quarter and ATVI's 29 to 59. Its weekly market value starts on 2021-09-03 for AAPL and cannot rank a session before it.
- Counts are restated to today's split basis: NVDA files 24.76 billion shares for early 2019, forty times its count then. So a value is the count times the close on the count's own split basis; the stored adjusted close also takes out dividends, which would understate a company paying 4 per cent a year by about a quarter over 2019's sessions against a company paying none.
- Two classes of one company share a filer: GOOGL and GOOG carry one CIK and each is filed with the count of every class, as FOXA and FOX and NWSA and NWS do, and BRK-B is filed in B-share equivalents. So companies are ranked by CIK with one listing held.
- Each company's GICS sector is served, departed ones included, as of the provider's last update and with no dated history. After the close of 2023-03-17 GICS moved 14 members of the index across five sectors, 8 from Information Technology to Financials, 3 from Information Technology to Industrials and 3 from Consumer Discretionary to Consumer Staples, so a session before the move reads each of the 14 in its earlier sector. The store's own sector labels are another scheme, and 184 of the 692 names the index held since 2018 carry none.
- All eleven sector funds are served, ten from 2018-01-02 over 2,200 sessions and XLC from its launch on 2018-06-19, so a look-back over the funds reads no Communication Services figure until late June 2019.
- Revenue as first reported is served by the SEC for every filer, departed ones included, each value with the date it was filed, and banks file theirs under a concept of their own. Over 484 prints of 16 names from 2019, the quarter's revenue was first filed on or before the report day for 106, 1 to 5 days after it for 117, 6 to 20 days after for 139 and later than that for 33, and 89, mostly fiscal fourth quarters, carry no quarterly value and are the year less its first nine months.
- The provider's revenue estimate is one figure a period with no date it was made on, so the size of a beat cannot be read as it stood before a report.
- The news archive reaches late 2017 for every sampled name, departed ones included, and is thin until 2021: eight mid-sized members held 150, 33, 60 and 91 articles in the half-years of 2019 and 2020, then 1,075, 1,149, 1,094 and 764 in the first halves of 2021 to 2024. Every article carries the provider's sentiment, four figures whose method its documentation does not state.
- The S&P 400 and 600 are served as today's members only, with none of the spans the S&P 500's answer carries; the provider's add-on holding twelve years of their changes costs $29.99 a month and refuses this key.

**The rulings of 2026-10-03.** The operator answered every question the evaluation raised; each answer the checkpoints below build on is a decision cited where it is built. The heavyweights' trade is scored in percent against the equal-weighted return of the size cut it was chosen from (see: A sector heavyweight's trade is scored by its percent return less the equal-weighted return of the size cut it was chosen from), with no market check of its own, and its readings are fixed one by one at 14.3: the leaders, what ends a holding, a year of 251 sessions, the sector's return, the rank by company, the value, the sector on a session, a member leaving the index and the beta. Revenue growth is read from the report date and checked against 30 sampled releases at 14.2. The size of a revenue beat is not tested, and five other checks are not adopted, each for the reason its decision states (see: Six context checks are not adopted, each for the reason it states). Analyst revisions join the pullback only at its go, as a carve-out with the sector leaders' rule retired to make room. The drift's ideas are judged by the year tests, the test without the five largest and 1,000 trades, with no floor of nights. At the heavyweights' freeze `CandidateEvaluators.cs` leaves the shared evaluation sources, its cost settled by the replay 14.1 builds.

**The fork, measured and brought to the operator.** Whether one trade a stock binds across all four cards (option A) or each card keeps its own (option B) is the page's rule and the operator's ruling, built at 14.3. Measured over 2019-01-02 to 2026-10-02 with the heavyweights replayed at their provisional settings on the ranks as they stood, every history name's company, counts and splits read from the provider for 7,612 weighted calls, against the page's swing picks drawn from the pullback's base, the breakout and the drift as frozen, five a family with one trade a stock across the three: the heavyweights made 438 trades, held a median 22 sessions, 368 ending where the stock no longer led and 66 at a close under its 200-day average. Under A, 112 of the 4,759 swing picks would have been hidden, 61 pullbacks, 27 breakouts and 24 drifts, and their results were better than the rest's, an average of 0.538 times the risk against 0.232 for the pullbacks, 0.342 against 0.112 for the breakouts and 0.275 against 0.220 for the drifts; and 82 of 478 heavyweight entries would have been held back to the next month. Under B, 65 stocks were on two cards at once on 1,238 of the 1,949 sessions, 2,218 stock-sessions in all and at most 6 on one session. The count is first-order: a hidden pick's place is not refilled from its family's next listing.

**The news check is not run.** The operator ruled it reads only the provider's sentiment, and only if that score is shown to be VADER's, a fixed word list scored over the article's text. Over 200 articles drawn evenly across 2019 to 2026, the best of six readings, VADER over the article's content, reproduced all four of the provider's figures to three decimals on 42 and the compound score alone on 134, where the bar was 190; every reading put 177 to 198 of the 200 on the same side of -0.05. So the score is not shown to be VADER's, and the check is among those not adopted.

**What a wider universe would take, reported for the operator to decide after this phase.** Membership history for the S&P 400 and 600 needs the provider's add-on at $29.99 a month: asked again on 2026-10-04 with historical=1, as the provider's documentation describes, the fundamentals answer for each still held today's members alone, 400 and 603, and no span of membership. Bars, sectors and quarterly figures are served for departed small and mid caps, and a departed name's ticker has to be checked against its company, since the provider serves another company's history under the ticker BBBY and the bankrupt retailer's under BBBYQ. The one-off pull would be about 24,000 weighted calls for 2,000 names, inside one day's allowance, and the quarters of 1,000 more members about 50,000 a year. Scaled from the night of 2026-10-02, 633 seconds to the close over 503 members, the night would reach the close in about 29 to 34 minutes against its 60-minute deadline, and the store would grow from 629 megabytes to about 1.5 to 1.9 gigabytes. A later phase would replay each swing family at its frozen settings on the 1,500 against the 500 over the eight years and widen a family only where its edge improves; the heavyweights stay on the 500. On the operator's ruling of 2026-10-04 a first test of it runs after this phase's report, at 14.8, on today's members of the 400 and the 600, whose history holds survivors only and so flatters the wider universe: where no family's edge improves even so, the widening is dropped and recorded as not adopted, and where one improves, nothing is adopted until the result is confirmed on membership as it stood, from the provider or from the iShares IJH and IJR holdings by date.

**What the phase 12 and 13 sign-off carried, and where each landed.** The sign-off entries of PR 333 name six items. The market series fetch reading an answer it cannot read as a series not served, the history pull's sibling, the guide's drift figure and the drain's claim comment landed as 13.10 and 12.5 corrections in PR 334. The drain's own stop landed as a 9.2 correction in PR 335. A family rule registered again is 14.1. The night of 2026-10-05 is read the morning after it and recorded in the entry after that night.

**Where the code lives.** The heavyweights' rule lives in files of their own, one rule shared by the night and the sweep and one stage after the families' that keeps the book. No line a pin reads is changed in a source it holds before the freeze, the family evaluator's and the candidate evaluators' and the swing filter's sources among them, and each entry states the pin lists read and that no pin moved, naming any pinned file whose comments alone it edited. At the freeze the evaluators' list leaves the shared evaluation sources in the same move that adds the heavyweights' evaluator, and the replay 14.1 builds states what that move restarts.

**One pull request a checkpoint.** On the operator's word of 2026-10-03, "Do not wait for me. Keep working on this until you finish the whole phase", each checkpoint lands as its own pull request, merged once both gates and the hosted runner are green and reported as it lands.

**What is the operator's.** The fork's ruling, before 14.3 builds the page's rule. The heavyweights' freeze and the registrations of Part B at 14.6, each on the operator's go. The add-on of Part C, decided after this phase's report and 14.8's test, and needed only where that test finds a family's edge improving and the membership as it stood is not read from the iShares holdings. No paid model call is made by any step of this phase without the operator's word.

**The operator's ruling on a name.** On 2026-10-03 the operator asked for the request queue's worker to be named for what it is, last of all before the report, and on 2026-10-04, before any of it was written, withdrew it: "you can skip the renaming". The worker keeps its names, and no checkpoint of this phase renames it.

**The claims phase 14 predicts.** 898 claims and 898 PASS before it. After 14.6 the pair is 958 and 958, within 940 to 975: none at 14.0, whose decisions and section 1's paragraph no table reads; about 3 at 14.1, the run page's line, the command's row and section 18's row for a replay that differs; about 8 at 14.2, the four pulled stores' rows and the readings each pull adds; about 32 at 14.3, the family's rule, its settings, its two stores, its component's two rows, section 18's four rows and the card's parts; about 6 at 14.4, each idea's row; about 3 at 14.5, the sweep's report; and about 8 at 14.6, the freeze's rows and the record's parts. 14.8 comes after the report, and the rows it adds are named beside the pair and never counted in it.

### 14.0 Planning
Writes this section, the decisions the phase rests on that describe no running code, the standing decision and the check that holds it, section 1's paragraph stating it, section 20's row for phase 14, and the captures the data checks read into the fixture with their manifest entries. Builds none of the phase.

**Done when** every decision the checkpoints below cite resolves, no done condition below waits on the calendar, each checkpoint below names the parts of the corpus it writes, section 20 carries one row for every phase this plan holds, every capture is declared in the manifest with what was cut from it, and `rules-choose-the-stocks` is on the roster, runs in `tools/ci.*`, states the components and files it read by count and by name, and fails over a constructed source that names a model's output table.

### 14.1 A family rule's record read by name, guarded by a replay
A family rule's record is read by its name from its first registration, as the pullback's rules' records are, and not from the registration it stands by. A command replays every family rule a change of code moves over the stored nights since its first registration, through the night's own evaluator and lister, and compares the trades it would have kept with the trades stored, the stock, the listing session, the buy, the stop, the target or trail, the end and the result, for exact equality: where every one is equal the rule's record carries on, and where one is not it restarts from the change, a run log row stating why and the run page saying so beside the rule. The remedy script runs it at every change that moves a family rule's code version and the family's registration command runs it before it registers, and every rule's result is stated on the run log and in the entry of the change. It is run once at landing over the move of 2026-10-03, which took the breakout's code version from 65ea06f28fff to a583427328fe and the drift's from 3309f7f59e9e to fc5b0bdd1256, and its result for every rule is stated. Writes the decision superseding the clause of the decision of 2026-10-03 that restarted every rule of both families, the old to Previously decided, section 15.10's line, section 18's row for a replay that differs, the command in `RUNBOOK.md` and its component's rows.

**Done when** a rule whose replay reproduces its stored trades is read from its first registration over constructed nights, one whose replay differs by one trade restarts from the change with its reason drawn, and a rule registered once is untouched; the replay over the move of 2026-10-03 is run from main's Release build and every rule's result is stated in the entry; and the entry states whether the record's reader sits in any pin list.

### 14.2 The pulls
`history-pull` gains four pulls, each into a table of its own marked by the pull that wrote it, removed whole by that pull and read by no night: each history name's company, CIK, GICS sector and delisting with its quarterly share counts and the dates their balance sheets were filed, one request a name; its splits, one a name; the eleven sector funds' daily series; and each filer's revenue as the SEC states it, every value with the date it was filed and its form. Each pull's try a name reads every failure but the run's own cancellation as a name not served, named and counted. Three readings shared by the night and the sweep are written in the core: a company's value on a session, the newest count filed before it times the close on its split basis; its sector on a session, with GICS's 14 moves of 2023-03-17 applied by date from a committed table the companies pull reads against the sectors and filers the provider files, refusing to land where it does not count 14; and a quarter's revenue as first filed, a fiscal fourth quarter the year less its first nine months. Writes migration 58, the four tables in section 16 and `SCHEMA.md`, the readings and the pulls in section 17 and section 18, the decisions for the value, the sector on a session and the rank by company, and the pull's lines in `RUNBOOK.md`; after the merge the migration and the pulls are run through the remedy script.

**Done when** the share count a session reads is worked by hand for a balance sheet filed the day before it and one filed the day after; a split between a filing and a session moves no value; a company paying dividends is valued as one paying none at the same close and count; two classes of one company are ranked once; a sector is read on both sides of 2023-03-17; revenue first filed is read where a later filing restates it, as a comparative column and for a fiscal fourth quarter; each pull over a constructed answer names a name not served and stores the rest; the remedy's entry records the names answered, those lacking a sector or a count, the splits, the revenue prints by concept and the check of 30 sampled releases against their filed revenue; and the fork's figures are stated again over the pulled data.

### 14.3 The sector heavyweights on screen, provisional
The night stores each member's company, CIK, sector and share count from the fundamentals answer it already asks for each member's reported quarters, with no request of its own, and values each member on the night at tonight's close brought to the count's basis. The heavyweights' rule, shared with the sweep, reads on the first session of each month the five largest companies of each sector by value, one listing a company, and buys the one leading its sector by most over 126 sessions, its sector's return the mean of its members' own, where that lead is above zero and the stock passes the trend gate; a holding ends at a month's start where the rule would not buy it, at any close under its 200-day average and at its last session as a member. A stage after the families keeps the book, storing each month's five a sector with their values, leads and trend and the leaders, and each holding with its entry, exit, reason, result in percent and the size cut's return over the same sessions. Tonight's page gains a fourth card: each holding with its sector, the day it was bought, its lead over its sector and its close against its 200-day average, what entered and left at the last rebalance, the next rebalance's date, held while leading in place of a stop and a target, and the provisional line. Past picks draws its trades in percent and a name's page says it is held. The family carries no market check, the fork's ruling sets whether one trade a stock binds across all four cards, the night's six reports take a new entry and never a holding carried from an earlier month, and the book joins the components `rules-choose-the-stocks` reads. Writes the family's rule in section 11, each part with its reason and its evidence, sections 14, 15.7, 15.9, 15.17, 16 and 17, its settings marked provisional, section 18's rows for a company with no sector filed, no count filed, a sector under five companies and a stock under 200 sessions, the catalogue's and the matrix's rows, `SCHEMA.md`, the decisions for the family and its readings, and the guide; migration 59, and the quarters run over every member through the remedy script after the merge.

**Done when** the size rank as it stood, the lead against the sector's equal-weighted return, the trend gate, both exits and the rebalance calendar, a first session that follows a holiday among them, are worked by hand over constructed members; a report is asked for an entry and never for a holding carried from an earlier month; the card is read back off the rendered page against the store in both directions; Past picks' percent is read back; and the entry states the pin lists read and that no pin moved, naming any pinned file whose comments alone it edited.

### 14.4 The context checks over the history, and their report
The ideas' run gains three ideas over the frozen families: the drift's revenue growth, from the quarter as first filed against the same quarter a year before, as a filter and as an order, judged by the year tests, the test without the five largest and 1,000 trades with no floor of nights; and the pullback's order by how far its 14-session relative strength fell from its high session, larger first, with three settings fixed before the run. Each is run from main's Release build, its figures recorded, and its report brought before anything registers. Writes each idea into section 13.9 with its reason and its evidence and no dated figure, and the decisions for the revenue's reading and the drift's floors.

**Done when** revenue growth is worked by hand over a constructed print, read from the first filing and never a later one; the relative strength's fall is worked by hand; and the report states each idea's tries against what luck passes.

### 14.5 The heavyweights' sweep
The sweep replays the heavyweights' rebalances over the history the pulls stored across 432 settings, the size cut, the look-back, the leaders a sector, the sector's return from its members or its fund, the beta, the rebalance's period and the exit, scored as the family is, one open trade a stock, and writes a report proposing the setting with the best edge among those with at least 300 trades and an edge above nothing in 6 of the 8 years, with every one-step neighbour, the record without the five largest, the trades a year and how long each is held, every member's and the index's return beside it, and what luck alone would pass.

**Done when** the percent edge and the walk are worked by hand over constructed rebalances; the replay's ranks, leads and leaders on sampled sessions equal the ones the night stored at 14.3; and the report's figures are read back against a constructed history with a known answer.

### 14.6 Freezes and registrations
On the operator's go: the heavyweights' rule and up to eight variants registered at one instant, each with its own book and its trades stored with their result and benchmark in percent, its record read on the run page over blocks of 63 sessions; the evaluators' list leaving the shared evaluation sources in the same move, with 14.1's replay run over every family rule and each result stated; the sector funds' closes asked for on the night only where a variant reading them registers; and Part B's registrations, the pullback's sector leaders' rule retired, the revisions variant registered with its carve-out and each passing idea registered where its family has room. One remedy.

**Done when** the command writes its rows at one instant or none; each variant is worked by hand on both sides of the setting it moves; a registered rule's record is read back off the run page over constructed trades; and the entry names every pinned file it edited and the remedy it issued.

### 14.7 Phase 14 report
`tools/verify-phase` reaches every claim this phase added, and the pair predicted above is checked against the actual with every claim that moved named; the add-on of Part C is put to the operator.

**Done when** the phase report is green with no claim unexamined and the pair is checked against the actual.

### 14.8 Part C's first test, on today's members
On the operator's ruling of 2026-10-04, after the report. `history-pull` pulls eight years of daily bars, splits and earnings dates for today's members of the S&P 400 and the S&P 600, the 1,003 names their fundamentals answers list, into tables of their own marked by the pull, removed whole by it and read by no night, inside the day's allowance, each name not served named and counted. The ideas' run replays each swing family at its frozen settings on the 1,500, those names beside the S&P 500's history as the store holds it, against the 500 alone, with the ideas' run's edge, pass test and luck figure. Every figure the report states for the 1,500 says that the 400's and 600's history holds survivors only, which flatters the wider universe. Where no family's edge improves on the 1,500 even so, the widening is dropped and recorded as not adopted; where one improves, nothing is adopted until the result is confirmed on membership as it stood, from the provider or from the iShares IJH and IJR holdings by date. Nothing is bought and no paid model call is made. Writes the pull's tables in section 16 and `SCHEMA.md`, its rows in sections 17 and 18, the replay's wider universe in section 13.9, the decision for the rule, and the pull and the run in `RUNBOOK.md`; after the merge the pull is run through the remedy script and the replay from main's Release build.

**Done when** the pull over constructed answers stores what was answered and names a name not served; a family's replay over a constructed history reads the 1,500 as the 500's history and the pulled members together and the 500 alone without them; every figure the report states for the 1,500 carries the words saying its 400 and 600 hold survivors only, read back off a constructed report; and the run's entry states each family's edge on both universes, its pass test and its luck figure, and whether the widening is dropped or waits on membership as it stood.

## Phase 15: the S&P 1500, each index with rules of its own

**Visible output at 15.1.** Tonight's page offers a choice of index, the S&P 500, 400 or 600, and the S&P 400 and 600 each draw their own cards, marked provisional.

**What this phase is for.** The operator's brief of 2026-10-04: the first test of the S&P 1500 showed the S&P 500's frozen settings do not carry to mid and small companies, and did not show that the S&P 400 and 600 cannot give good picks (see: The S&P 400 and 600 join the universe with rules of their own, since the first test showed only that the 500's settings do not carry to them). The night reads the S&P 500 and today's members of the S&P 400 and 600, each member tagged with its index, and every family runs on the 400 and the 600 as rules of their own, with settings, variants, records and checkpoints of their own: each index's strength ranked, market check read and edge measured among its own members, every 400 and 600 trade paying its cost, and the S&P 500's rules staying as frozen. Evaluate first, verify the data, then plan, and report before building; rules choose the stocks, and no paid spend without the operator's word.

**What the evaluation found.** The first test's figures are over a mixed population, one pool, one strength rank and one benchmark across three indices, so they answer whether the 500's settings carry over and not whether the 400 or 600 can give good picks. Over 1,949 nights, survivors only: the pullback's base 0.100 over 1,903 trades on the 500 against -0.017 over 5,354 on the 1,500, the breakout 0.082 over 1,009 against 0.067 over 2,454, the earnings drift 0.121 over 2,243 against -0.222 over 3,809. They stand as the evidence that each index needs its own settings, and the split of the frozen settings by index is the first row of each index's sweep at 15.4, labelled survivors only. The literature each rule of this phase rests on is in section 11.4, each finding with its source and what is judgement. Where it ran against the brief it was put to the operator and ruled: drift after an earnings surprise has gone outside microcaps (Martineau 2022), and the S&P 400 and 600 hold none, so the drift's card carries that evidence (see: The 400's and 600's earnings drift lists from the first night with the evidence against it on its card); the lead of big firms over small ones within an industry runs mainly through bad news (Hou 2007), so the followers' design rests on its weaker side and a swing rule may test the stronger (see: A 400 or 600 swing pick may be kept off where its industry's S&P 500 heavyweights fell, a dial its sweeps test); and analysts' coverage helps momentum mostly among losers (Hong, Lim and Stein 2000) and has no dated history, so it is stored and not swept (see: Analyst coverage is stored from each fetch and waits for dated counts before any rule tests it).

**What the data checks found, on 2026-10-04 and 2026-10-05.** The store was opened read-only; the provider's answers cost 306 of the day's 100,000 weighted calls and the SEC's and iShares' none, and the captures behind each finding are in the fixture's manifest under the prefix `phase15-`.
- The night's time. The last four scheduled nights reached the close in 580, 616, 640 and 701 seconds over 503 names; scaled by 1,506 over 503, a night over the three indices reaches it in about 29 to 35 minutes, against the wall clock of 40 minutes and the deadline of 2 hours the operator ruled for it.
- The store's growth. 929.5 megabytes on 2026-10-04; a night writes about 8.5 megabytes of rows over 503 names, gate results 2.18, family results 3.77, version scores 0.95, fundamental readings 0.55, listings 0.46, peer readings 0.41 and the rest 0.14, so about 25 a night and 6.4 gigabytes a year of rows over 1,506, and about 223 megabytes once for the 1,003 names' year of bars, indicators, levels, profiles and ladders.
- The four funds. SPY, IJH, IJR and HYG are each served over 2,200 sessions from 2018-01-02 to 2026-10-02, their adjusted closes carrying their payouts: HYG's adjusted close stood at 0.627 of its raw close on its first session, so its three-month change is read on adjusted closes.
- The provider's member lists. Against the funds' own holdings as of 2026-10-01, 9 of the 400's and 33 of the 600's tickers differ, most of them renames the provider never took up, GPS where the company trades as GAP, NYCB as FLG and RCII as UPBD, and 42 of the 1,003 names it listed on 2026-10-04 hold no pulled bar since 2026-09-25 (see: The S&P 400's and 600's members are read each night from their funds' own holdings files).
- The quarters. Over 30 members drawn with a stated seed, 15 an index, every answer carries a GICS sector and industry, a CIK and quarterly income statements with the dates they were filed, net income on every quarter since 2018 and interest expense on every quarter for 27 of the 30; 6 sum to a loss over their four newest quarters, 2 of 15 on the 400 and 4 of 15 on the 600, where the provider's own earnings a share showed 11 of 399 and 39 of 595; 9 cover their interest under twice, 3 of them banks. The SEC's facts for the same filers carry net income for all 30 and operating income for 25, and the interest expense under several concepts that change over the years, 12 of the 30 tagging it on fewer than 10 quarters, so the history reads net income as the SEC first filed it and the coverage from the provider's quarters by the dates they were filed.
