- Membership as it stood. iShares serves its funds' holdings for today alone: the IJR page offered 2026-10-01, 2026-09-30 and 2025-12-31, and the dated file answered the product page for 2019-01-31 and 2022-12-30. The SEC holds each fund's quarter-end holdings, IJR as series S000004313 and IJH as S000004307, 28 public N-PORT filings each from the quarter to 2019-09-30, with a CUSIP and an ISIN for each holding and no ticker. The rule stated before the run, at least 98 per cent of each sampled snapshot's equity holdings matched to the provider's symbols, failed by ISIN alone: 92.5 per cent of IJR's 604 and 95.3 of IJH's 401 on 2019-09-30, 98.0 and 99.0 on 2024-12-31, the provider carrying an ISIN on 18,803 of its 60,411 delisted US symbols. The company's name as a second key reached 98.7 to 99.5 on all four (see: Membership as it stood is rebuilt from the funds' quarterly holdings filed with the SEC, matched by ISIN and then by name).
- The floors and the gate over today's members, survivors only. On 2026-10-02 the floors leave out none of 363 400 members read and 7 of 541 600 members, 4 of them under $5; on 2019-06-28, 23 of 324 and 111 of 480. Each index's own breadth stood at or above 0.5 on 76 per cent of the 1,949 nights for the 400 and 65 for the 600, 27 and 16 in 2022, beside the 500's 73.
- Costs. Over 2025-09-26 to 2026-09-25 the estimators read from daily bars put the median member's round trip at 0.663, 0.790 and 0.877 per cent by Corwin and Schultz, 0.390, 0.489 and 0.444 by Abdi and Ranaldo and 0.348, 0.500 and 0.514 by EDGE, against an S&P 500 company's measured 0.03; at a stop 1.5 typical moves beneath, 0.17 to 0.19 of a trade's risk by Corwin and Schultz, and about 0.008, 0.015 and 0.021 by the published table (see: A 400 or 600 trade pays the published effective spread for its size and price, and its pass tests read the edge after it).

**The rulings of 2026-10-04 and 2026-10-05.** The operator answered four questions with the plan, reviewed it with two corrections, and answered two after the data checks, each word for word in `prompts/`. Costs come from the published table and not from an estimator, and come off the trade alone (see: A trade's cost comes off the trade and not its benchmark). The 400's and 600's drift lists from the first night with the evidence on its card. Phase 14's sign-off is owed after this phase's plan, done by a session that committed none of phase 14's code, beside this phase, and no checkpoint of phase 15 discharges it. The night's wall clock is 40 minutes and its deadline two hours, three times it. No family on any index is set aside, retired or hidden by a test result without the operator's word (see: No family on any index is set aside or hidden by a test result without the operator's word). The night's six reports are taken in turn across the indices, one at a time in the order S&P 500, S&P 400, S&P 600, repeating, so each index has two a night when it has picks, the page's family order within an index and an index with no picks left passing its turn to the next. The history's membership is rebuilt free on two keys, and the night reads the 400's and 600's members from their funds' files. The decisions that change what runs today, the universe, the market check, the six reports, the deadline and the news labeller, are written where they are built, at 15.1. Ruling 15.1's second half on 2026-10-05, the operator chose each index's provisional picks computed on the night by the sweep's own code into tables of their own over the night's S&P 500 stages widened, and every page reading one index at a time chosen under Universe with every figure naming its index.

**Where the code lives.** The 400's and 600's rules live in files of their own that call the frozen rules' functions, so no line a pin reads moves in a source the S&P 500's rules hold, and each entry states the pin lists read and that no pin moved, naming any pinned file whose comments alone it edited. 15.0 edits the comments of pinned files alone, the two decisions renamed at 15.0 cited by their new names.

**One pull request a checkpoint.** On the operator's order of 2026-10-04: one pull request at a time, each reported after it merges, the evaluation, the literature check and the data checks first, then the nightly 1,500 with its provisional rules and the choice of index, the readings and the costs, the sweeps per index and family with their reports, and the freezes on the operator's go.

**What is the operator's.** Each freeze, per index and family. Setting aside, retiring or hiding any family on any index. The provider's add-on for membership as it stood, bought only on the operator's explicit yes. Widening the news labeller to the 400's and 600's lists. No paid call is made by any step of this phase without the operator's word.

**The claims phase 15 predicts.** 973 claims and 973 PASS before it. After 15.5 the pair is 1,049 and 1,049, within 1,020 to 1,080: none at 15.0, which rewords section 17's family sweep floors and section 18's family sweep with no setting meeting its floors and adds no row; about 30 at 15.1, the membership of three indices and its failure rows, the funds' files, each index's strength, breadth and market check, the provisional rules' floors and gate, the six reports in turn, the choice of index on five screens and the night's limits; about 18 at 15.2, each reading's row and the cost's; about 8 at 15.3, the pulls' rows and membership as it stood; about 12 at 15.4, each index's sweep rows, the no-pass card's line and its failure rows; and about 8 at 15.5, the freezes' rows and the records' parts.

### 15.0 Planning
Writes this section, the decisions the phase rests on that describe no running code, section 11.4's evidence and section 13.9's paragraph after the first test, section 20's row for phase 15 and section 23's sources, the captures the data checks read into the fixture with their manifest entries, and the report half of the no-drop ruling: a sweep no setting of which meets the floors proposes nothing and states the five strongest settings it read, each with the floors it falls short of and by how much, and what could be tried next, each dial the strongest reads at an end of its grid, the floors they miss and the ideas' run's tests that fit the family, saying a sweep cannot tell which of those have run. The two decisions whose words set such a family aside are written again without them, every citation moving to the new names. Builds none of the phase's night.

**Done when** every decision the checkpoints below cite resolves, no done condition below waits on the calendar, each checkpoint below names the parts of the corpus it writes, section 20 carries one row for every phase this plan holds, every capture is declared in the manifest with what was cut from it, and a constructed history no setting of which meets the floors draws the five strongest with their shortfalls and what could be tried next on both the family's and the heavyweights' reports, the words "set aside" nowhere on either.

### 15.1 The nightly 1,500 with provisional rules and a choice of index
The night reads the S&P 500's members from the provider and the S&P 400's and 600's from their funds' holdings files, one request a fund and no weight on the allowance, a file it cannot read keeping the members of the night before and saying so on the run page (see: The S&P 400's and 600's members are read each night from their funds' own holdings files). A 400 or 600 span opens on the first night its fund lists the name and closes on the night it stops, a name moving between indices closes one span and opens another the same night, and the unlisted guard is 2 per cent of the index's members, 10, 8 and 12, proposed and settled by the first year's nights. Each member's rows carry its index; the stages a name is computed in run over the three; strength is ranked and breadth read among each index's own members; a row is written for every member of each index every night, the S&P 500's in the listings and the 400's and 600's in tables of their own; and one trade a stock holds across every card of every index. SPY, IJH, IJR and HYG join the series the night asks for beside the index and the VIX, one request each, and `history-pull` gains them from 2018. Every family runs on the 400 and the 600 as a rule of its own on provisional settings, read each night over the year of bars the night stored by the code its sweep reads it with and written into tables of their own (see: The 400's and 600's provisional picks are computed on the night by the sweep's own code into tables of their own), its card's status line reading "Provisional: not yet frozen" and its record starting at its freeze (see: The 400 and 600 rules start provisional with liquidity floors and a profit gate before any testing): the pullback's base, the breakout and the drift as frozen and the heavyweights on design (a) at the 500's frozen settings within the index, its sector's return its members' mean and its beta read against the index's fund (see: The 400 and 600 each sweep two heavyweight designs and keep the stronger after costs), each with the $5 floor, the dollar volume floor of $10 million on the 400 and $5 million on the 600, the profit gate and the index's own breadth at the 500's floor. Tonight, Past picks, Run, Universe and Researched open with a selector labelled Universe offering the S&P 500, 400 and 600, each with its member count, kept in the address so a link opens it and the S&P 500 where it names none, and every figure on a page names the index it is over, none mixing two (see: Every page reads one index at a time chosen under Universe, and every figure names its index); each index draws its own cards in the page's order, each card's description written by code from its index's own rule settings and stating on the 400 and 600 the minimum price, the dollar volume floor, the profit check and the costs taken off, the drift's card on the 400 and 600 carrying its line of evidence and the heavyweights' naming its design and its index's sector comparison; and the "Still open" line and Past picks name the index each trade was listed under. The night's six reports are taken in turn across the indices as ruled. The night's wall clock becomes 40 minutes and its deadline two hours; the news labeller reads the S&P 500's list alone until the operator widens it; the overnight queue reads tonight's lists first in the choice of index's order. Before the first night the remedy runs the migration, the 1,003 names' one-year backfill, about 1,003 weighted calls, their first quarters, about 11,000, and the four funds' history. Writes the decisions for the universe, the market check of each index, the six reports in turn, the deadline and the labeller, each superseding the entry it replaces; CLAUDE.md's hard rules for three indices with the prior text in `CHANGELOG.md`; sections 11.4, 14, 15.7, 15.8, 15.10, 15.17, 16, 17 and 18; `SCHEMA.md`; the runbook; and the guide (owes: The night's time and growth over the three indices read on its first five nights).

**15.1 lands in three pull requests, each with its own entry and its part of the done condition.** On the operator's order of 2026-10-05 that 15.1 merge that day with the first night over the three indices that night: the first is the night, the three indices' membership, every stage that stores or computes what a member needs reading the three, the four funds' series and their history, the wall clock and the deadline, each index's members on the Run page's night line, and the remedy that loads the new members' years and quarters before their first night; the second, on its own branch after it, is each index's provisional rules read on the night by its sweep's own code into tables of their own and its cards, strength and breadth within each index, one trade a stock across every card, the choice of index, the six reports in turn and the labeller, with no S&P 500 rule, stage or pin moving and the ladder not widened, as the operator ruled on 2026-10-05; and the third, after it, is the overnight queue's order across the three indices, which lands once the first night shows what each new member's facts file holds, since the queue judges each name off its facts file and no pass has yet read an S&P 400 or 600 member's. Until the second lands the S&P 400's and 600's members are stored and computed and listed by nothing, and until the third the queue reads the S&P 500's members alone.

**Done when**, for the night, the first pull request: a constructed night stores three indices' members from a provider answer and two funds' files, keeps the night before's members where a file cannot be read, and moves a name between indices, a name the S&P 500 holds read as its member alone; each fund's guard holds at its figure and closes none one past it; every stage storing or computing what a member needs reads the three indices' members and every stage ranking, listing or recording reads the S&P 500's alone; the Run page draws each index's members beside the time to the close; the remedy's entry records the first night's time to the close against 40 minutes and two hours, the rows written per index and the requests and weighted calls; and the entry states the pin lists read and that no pin moved. And for the cards, the second: a name moving between indices keeps its open trade; strength and breadth are worked by hand within each index; each floor and the profit gate are worked by hand on both sides, a member with three quarters filed failing it; each index's picks over a constructed night are the ones its sweep's code reads at the provisional setting, every member's answer stored whether it passed or not; each index's cards are read back off the rendered page against the store in both directions, and every page is screenshotted under each of the three choices and read back, its heading, every count and each card's description naming the right index and drawn from that index's settings; a test changes a 400 rule's setting and finds its card's description changed; the six reports are taken in turn over constructed nights, two an index where each has picks, an index with none passing its turns and one with a single pick passing its second; Past picks and the "Still open" line name each trade's index; and its entry states the pin lists read and that no pin moved. And for the queue, the third: over a constructed night the overnight queue takes the S&P 500's list, then the S&P 400's and then the S&P 600's, each in its page's order, before every other member, the S&P 500's first among those; and a pass over an S&P 400 member's facts file as the night stores it writes its sections.

### 15.2 The readings and the costs
Every member of the three indices gains, as it stood on the session: its dollar volume, the mean of close times volume over 50 sessions; a trade's cost, half the published spread of its value band and price band at entry and half at exit, stated at the table's value and at double; its quality at four levels, the profit gate, interest coverage of at least 2 over the four newest quarters with a company filing no interest expense passing and GICS Financials read as not applicable, and the state its reported quarters read not deteriorating; its nearness to the 251 sessions' highest high and the sessions since it; its volume over its 50-session average; its GICS industry's S&P 500 members' value-weighted return over 21 and 63 sessions, none where the industry holds no S&P 500 member, and the value-weighted mean surprise of those reporting in the 20 sessions before a reaction; IJR's and IJH's adjusted closes against SPY's over 126 and 252 sessions; HYG's adjusted close against its 50-session average and its 63-session change; and the five rating counts summed from each fundamentals answer, dated by the fetch, the run page stating how many of the 400's and 600's members hold four dated counts (owes: Analyst coverage tested as a dial once dated counts exist). The 400's and 600's records store each trade's result before and after its cost; the S&P 500's rules show the edge after costs beside their frozen figures, their records unchanged. Each reading is one function the night and the sweep share. Writes sections 11.4, 15.9, 15.10, 16, 17 and 18, `SCHEMA.md` and the guide.

**15.2 lands in two pull requests, each with its own entry and its half of the done condition.** On the operator's order of 2026-10-05 that the sweeps start as soon as the readings they need are built: the first is the readings the sweeps read, the dollar volume and the floors, a trade's cost and the profit gate and the coverage, each one function, with each pulled company's quarterly income statements as filed, which the companies pull stores from the answer it already asks for and which move here from 15.3 since the gate reads them; the second is every reading stored for every member every night, the rest of the readings, the records before and after costs, the name page and the run page's count of dated ratings.

**Done when**, for the readings the sweeps read, the first pull request: the dollar volume and each floor are worked by hand at a constructed session, at their figures and a step under them; the profit gate and the coverage are worked by hand as they stood, a quarter filed on the session or after it not read; the cost is worked by hand in every value band and price band and at double, a company with no count read in its band; the companies pull over constructed answers stores each quarter's income as filed and names a company filing none; and the entry states the pin lists read and that no pin moved. And for the rest, the second: each remaining reading is worked by hand at a constructed session as it stood; the edge after costs is the edge before it less the trade's own round trip; the S&P 500's records are read back unchanged; and its entry states the pin lists read and that no pin moved.

### 15.3 The history for the sweeps
`history-pull` gains, each into a table of its own marked by the pull that wrote it, removed whole by it and read by no night: each fund's quarter-end holdings from its N-PORT filings, the 28 from the quarter to 2019-09-30, and before them from the N-Q for 2018-12-31 and the annual report for 2019-03-31, each one document of schedules shared by dozens of the trust's funds naming each holding with no identifier, read for the fund's complete schedule (see: The funds' holdings before their first public N-PORT are read from their N-Q of 2018-12-31 and their annual report of 2019-03-31); each holding matched to the provider's symbols by ISIN and then by name, a name match kept only where its code traded at the quarter's end (see: A holding matched by name is kept only where its code traded at the quarter's end), a holding matched by neither listed, and a quarter an earlier pull stored matched again under the rule as it stands; the bars, prints, surprises and splits of every name the snapshots hold that the 1,003 do not, each ticker checked against its company; each 400 and 600 history name's company, CIK, GICS industry, share counts and quarterly income statements as filed, through the companies pull 15.2 widened; and each filer's net income as the SEC first filed it. Membership as it stood is a name held from the first snapshot holding it to the last. Writes sections 13.9, 16, 17 and 18, `SCHEMA.md` and the runbook; after the merge the pulls run through the remedy script.

**Done when** each pull over constructed answers stores what was answered and names what was not served; a name is read as a member on both sides of a quarter-end its snapshots straddle; a name matched by ISIN, one by name alone and one by neither are each stated; a name whose codes did not trade at the quarter's end, or traded only at a close more than the stated tolerance from the fund's value a share, is matched to none, a name nothing matched is read more widely, and a quarter an earlier pull stored is matched again; the N-Q's and the annual report's captured pages are read for each fund's complete schedule and a summary alone is refused; the remedy's entry records the snapshots read, the holdings matched by each key and by neither, and 30 name matches checked by hand, and the correction's remedy entry the same 30 checked again.

### 15.4 The sweeps per index and family
Each swing family is swept on the S&P 400 and on the S&P 600, its strength, market check and benchmark read on that index alone and every result after costs, labelled as read on membership as it stood or on survivors only, both shown where both exist: first its grid at the provisional floors and gate, the breakout's 54 settings, the drift's 81 and the pullback's nine dials by the sweep's own search; then each new dial tried alone on its ten best settings and kept where the edge after costs rises in 6 of the 8 years with 2 of the last 3, the floor of dollar volume at half, once and twice its provisional value, the price at $5 or $10, the quality's four levels, the hold at 21, 42 and 63 sessions, the industry's fall over 21 sessions, over 63 or off, and for the breakout the nearness to the year's high at any, 0.95 or 1.0, the recency of the year's high at any, within 63 sessions or older, and a volume multiple of 1.25, 1.5, 2.0 or 3.0, and for the drift a window of 3, 5, 10 and 20 sessions as four levels, and its peer reading; then the survivors crossed. The index switches, small beating large and credit, and the S&P 500's breadth in place of the index's own, are judged on the year's total result as the ideas' run judges a switch. The heavyweights are swept on each index in two designs, (a) over 864 settings and (b) over 64, each scored in percent against its sector's equal-weighted members in the index after costs, the stronger meeting the family sweeps' floors kept. The trust checks are as before: the floors, the plateau's margin of 0.05, depth, one-step neighbours, the edge without the five largest, year by year, and what luck alone passes over the tries. A family no setting of which meets the floors on an index keeps listing there on its provisional settings, and a command run after its sweep records that answer, the card's one line reading "Its sweep found no setting that passed the floors" until the family's next freeze or sweep (see: No family on any index is set aside or hidden by a test result without the operator's word). Writes sections 13.9, 15.7, 17 and 18 and the runbook; each run from main's Release build, its figures in its own entry and its report brought to the operator.

**Done when** each index's walk and its after-cost edge are worked by hand over constructed histories; a constructed history's report is read back with its known answer; the card's line is drawn after the recorded answer and gone after a freeze, read off the rendered page; and each run's entry states each family's figures on each index, on membership as it stood and on survivors only, and what luck passes.

### 15.5 Freezes and registrations
On the operator's go for each index and family: its rule and up to eight variants registered at one instant, each with its own record and checkpoints counted among that family's own rules on that index, at most nine, and the remedy that registers them. One remedy for each go.

**15.5 lands in two pull requests, each with its own entry and its half of the done condition.** The operator ruled on 2026-10-05 that no family on the S&P 400 or 600 is frozen yet, so 15.5 builds the freeze command and issues no remedy: the first pull request is the three swing families' freezes on each index, each rule read on the night at its registration's settings with its levels read off the readings the night stored, keeping its own list and trades, a family's live rule drawing its card and its list, and each rule's record on the index's Run page; the second, on its own branch after it, is the sector heavyweights' freeze on each index, each rule keeping a book of its own as the S&P 500's registered heavyweights rules do.

**Done when**, for the swing families, the first pull request: the command writes its rows at one instant or none, refusing a setting its index's sweep did not read; each variant is worked by hand on both sides of the setting it moves; a freeze of a family on the S&P 400 or 600 takes its card's line saying its sweep found no setting that passed away from that night on and not before, read off the rendered page; each rule keeps its own list and trades over a constructed night, a rule the night cannot read named; and the entry names every pinned file it edited and the remedy it issued, or that the operator gave no go and none was issued. And for the heavyweights, the second: the command writes the live rule and its variants at one instant or none; each variant is worked by hand beside the live rule on both sides of the setting it moves; each rule's book is its own, its holdings read back against the store; a freeze takes the heavyweights' card's line away from that night on and not before, read off the rendered page; and its entry names every pinned file it edited and the remedy it issued, or that none was issued.

### 15.6 Phase 15 report
`tools/verify-phase` reaches every claim this phase added, and the pair predicted above is checked against the actual with every claim that moved named.

**Done when** the phase report is green with no claim unexamined and the pair is checked against the actual.

## Phase 16: the decision card, what a buy needs gathered beside each pick

**Visible output at 16.1.** Each pick on Tonight opens its card in place beneath its row, for every family on every index, and a stock's page opens with the card of the family it is listed under that night: the checklist's lines, each a tick or a warning with its reason in words, and the record of the rule that picked it under the rule's own heading.

**What this phase is for.** The operator's brief of 2026-10-05: the research report explains the company and the chart, and tonight's page says which stocks the rules picked, and neither answers what a decision needs: whether anything is against the trade, what the rule that picked it has done before, how many shares to buy and what that risks, what could gap it before it ends, and how the operator's own picks like it have done. Most of the figures are already in the store, spread over several pages. The card gathers them, one a pick, every figure computed by code, read as it stood on the night and naming its index, advising on the trade and never removing, reordering or hiding a pick (see: A pick's card advises on the trade and removes no pick, and code computes every figure on it). Rules choose the stocks, no model output enters the card, and the research report is unchanged.

**What the evaluation found.** The code, the corpus and the live store, opened read-only, were read against the brief, and the outside claims against their sources. Most of the brief stands; five parts of it ran into standing rules or the store's contents, and three of those change the design.
- A rule's record beside a stock runs into the hard rule that a reason's record is shown beside the reason and never beside the ticker, because beside a ticker it would be read as that stock's chance of going up. The operator ruled that the card draws it under the rule's own heading, naming the rule and its index and never the stock, with the line "This is the rule's record over every stock it bought, not this stock's chance", so 16.1 rewrites the hard rule and the decision behind it, the prior text to `CHANGELOG.md` and the decision's 15 citations moved.
- A screen reads and renders and computes nothing, by a decision cited 46 times in 21 files, and the plan in the operator's money can never be stored, since the account settings must never be and the shares times the stop's distance gives the dollars at risk back. So 16.2 writes the one exception, the plan worked out where the card is drawn by one function in the core, and moves the 46 citations to it.
- The sweep stores neither the worst dip nor the holding time: its figures for each setting are the twelve a year its measures hold, in files under the sweep's own folder and not in the store; the register's evidence column is empty on all 126 registrations; and the S&P 500's sweeps of phases 12 to 14 ran before costs. Copied from the sweep, two of the record's four figures would be dashed on every card and "after costs" would not hold on the S&P 500, so each rule's record is replayed at its one setting by the sweep's own code and stored with all four figures (see: A rule's record is replayed at its one setting by the sweep's own code over the pulled history, after costs on every index). The settings were chosen on that same history, so the record reads high, and the card says so (Bailey, Borwein, López de Prado and Zhu 2014).
- Earnings inside the longest hold would warn on nearly every pick. The caps are 63 sessions for the pullback and the breakout and 60 for the drift, about a quarter, and a company reports once a quarter: over 18,036 S&P 500 member-nights, 36 nights from 2025-10-09 to 2026-06-23, the next report fell within 63 sessions on 96.0 per cent, within 60 on 93.6, within 20 on 31.2 and within 15 on 24.1. So the line warns inside the time by which three in four of the rule's trades had ended (see: The card warns of a report inside the time three in four of the rule's trades had ended by, and notes one inside the cap alone).
- Earnings gaps are large against these stops: over 1,916 stored S&P 500 reports the reaction session's close moved a median 1.3 typical moves, 44.8 per cent at least 1.5, the breakout's stop distance, 33.1 at least 2 and 19.0 at least 3, and every rule here sells on a close through its stop, so a report inside the hold can carry a trade past its stop in one session. The card states the stock's own reactions in the plan's risks.
- Every stop is a closing rule: the pullback and the drift are stopped on a close below the stop and the breakout's trail is read on closes, so a resting stop order with a broker fills during the session and is a different trade from the one the record measured. The card's management says to sell at the close of a session that closes below the stop.
- The heavyweights have no stop: they are held while they lead and sold at a month's first close where the rule would no longer buy them, so the dollars at risk over a stop's distance has no distance, and their record is in percent against their size cut (see: A holding with no stop is sized at an equal share of the account across the most holdings its book can hold).
- Ex-dividend dates: the stored fundamentals carry the last declared ex-date as of the fetch that wrote them, and the provider's dividend calendar answers a day at a time, one request each. Over 398 payers a quarterly dividend is a median 0.19 typical moves, 0.13 risks at a stop 1.5 typical moves beneath and 0.36 risks at the 90th percentile: small for most names and material for the high yielders.
- FOMC decisions and CPI releases: announcement days carry much higher market returns and Sharpe ratios (Savor and Wilson 2013). The Fed's own page carries the 2026 and 2027 meetings and the BLS has not yet published 2027's CPI dates; the provider's economic calendar would be matched by event names it can change without notice (see: Market events inside a hold are read from a committed table of the Fed's and the BLS's own dates, and asked of no provider).
- Concentration crosses indices: over the stored year the mean pairwise correlation of daily returns among 497 S&P 500 members is 0.254 within a sector and 0.104 across sectors. The operator ruled that the line counts the open taken trades in the stock's sector across all three indices, the one figure reading every index, which 16.2 writes as a decision narrowing the one that every figure names its index.
- The S&P 400's and 600's stores: before the night of 2026-10-06 the calendar, the earnings reactions, the fundamental state and the swing readings held S&P 500 names alone, the calendar fetcher and the fundamental reader widened at 15.1 after the last night had run, so that night is the first to fill them. The swing reader stays the S&P 500's, so the card reads each index's sector ranks itself, and the 400's and 600's passed rows store the rule's settings and no gate values, so the first line there names the gates in words without their values.
- The first line is a tick on every card, since a pick passed every gate to be listed, so it is drawn as words, "passed every gate", and no warning is invented for it.
- Each checkpoint lands its own corpus: `writer-ownership`, `component-access` and `decision-resolves` refuse code that writes a table, adds a component or cites a decision the corpus does not declare, so the documents cannot all wait for the last checkpoint, which keeps the guide's section, the figure, the screenshots and the report.

**What the data check found, on 2026-10-06.** The provider's dividend calendar was asked for each of the 21 sessions from 2026-10-07 to 2026-11-04, one request a session at a weight of one, 21 of the day's 100,000 weighted calls, every answer on one page carrying a symbol and a date and nothing else: 917 rows over 847 US listings. The store was opened read-only. Of the S&P 500's 397 payers, a forward rate above nothing in each one's newest snapshot, 44 name a declared ex-date after the night and 29 of those fall inside the window; the calendar carries all 29 on the date declared, 29 of 44 counting the 15 declared beyond the window as missed, so the calendar stands as the line's source. The S&P 400's and 600's members hold two snapshots between them, neither a payer's, since the fundamentals fetcher runs when a name is opened. The calendar dates 42 of the 397 inside the window, 43 of the S&P 500's 503 members, 20 of the 400's and 32 of the 600's, because a date reaches it only once the company declares it: a quarter on from each payer's last declared ex-date puts about 30 more of the 397 inside the window undeclared. So the line draws the calendar's declared date where it carries one and, for a payer it carries none for, the date estimated from the company's own schedule, its last declared ex-date plus its usual interval, drawn "estimated", the fallback the operator ruled. The fundamentals answer the quarters fetch already asks for each member carries the last ex-date, the forward rate and each year's count of dividends, so once that fetch keeps them the estimate and the amount cost no request. The 21 answers are kept in the main checkout's `prompts/`, and 16.3 captures one into the fixture before its reader is written.

**The rulings of 2026-10-05 and 2026-10-06.** The operator approved the plan on 2026-10-05 with R1 to R10 as recommended and two changes, and answered four questions on 2026-10-06, each word for word in `prompts/`. The card's values are the plan's: a warning where the stock's sector stands in the bottom 3 of the index's 11, where the round trip costs 0.10 risks or more, where 2 or more of the operator's open trades share its sector, where interest cover is under 2, and where a report falls inside the time by which three in four of the rule's trades had ended; and a position capped at a fifth of the account. All of them sit in one settings block the operator can change, and each card stores the values it was read with. The operator's own record states its average result once 20 of its trades in that family and index have ended (see: The operator's own record states its average result once twenty of its trades in a family and index have ended). The account settings live in a file of their own (see: The account settings live in a file of their own under the data root and in nothing the store or the logs hold). A taken trade's fill is the next session's open (see: A taken trade's fill is the next session's open once its bar is stored, and the plan's buy marked provisional until then). Phase 14's, phase 15's and phase 16's sign-offs are owed after 16.4, each done by a fresh session the operator starts, one at a time and none beside the build, since a review's branch and its mutations would share the one checkout the build runs in; no checkpoint of phase 16 discharges any of them, and each is handed its prompt after 16.4 with the figures as they stand then. Each pull request merges without a go once its gates and the hosted runs are green. No paid call is made and nothing is bought; provider calls are stated before each run and held under the day's allowance.

**Where the code lives.** The card's code lives in files of its own in the core, the worker, the read surface and the pages, calling the rules' own functions, so no line a pin reads moves in a source any rule holds, and each entry states the pin lists read and that no pin moved. The record replays a rule with the sweep's own walk and edits none of it.

**One pull request a checkpoint.** 16.1 to 16.4 in order, each reported after it merges, and 16.5 after the report on the operator's ruling of 2026-10-07, in one pull request with the corrections the screens read back found.

**What is the operator's.** The account settings, entered on the settings page and held nowhere else. Each Taken press and each exit. The card's values, changed in its settings block. Any paid call. The three sign-offs, each by a fresh session the operator starts.

**The claims phase 16 predicts.** 1,000 claims and 1,000 PASS before it. After 16.4 the pair is 1,040 and 1,040, within 1,030 to 1,052: none at 16.0, which adds section 20's row and no row of a table the harness reads; about 16 at 16.1, the card's rows, its two tables, the record's command, four failure rows and five thresholds; about 10 at 16.2, the table of taken trades, the settings file, the position cap, the presses and two failure rows; about 12 at 16.3, the follower, the events table, the dividend ask, three failure rows and the operator's record's threshold; and about 2 at 16.4, the card's figure and its key. 16.5, after the report, adds no row of a table the harness reads: its words go to rows that stand.

### 16.0 Planning
Writes this section, the decisions the phase rests on that describe no running code, section 20's row for phase 16 and the phase's carried obligation, and runs the data check above. The decisions that change what runs today are written where they are built: the records drawn beside a pick at 16.1, the screen's one computation and the concentration line reading every index at 16.2, and the night's ex-dividend ask at 16.3, each superseding or narrowing the entry it changes. Section 23's sources are written with the paragraphs that cite them, Moskowitz and Grinblatt (1999) and Bailey, Borwein, López de Prado and Zhu (2014) at 16.1 and Savor and Wilson (2013) at 16.3, since `research-marked` refuses a source no rule cites. Builds none of the phase's card.

**Done when** every decision the checkpoints below cite resolves, no done condition below waits on the calendar, each checkpoint below names the parts of the corpus it writes, section 20 carries one row for every phase this plan holds, and the data check's counts are recorded with the requests they cost.

### 16.1 The card's frame, the checklist's first five lines and the rule's record
A night step after the S&P 400's and 600's families and the books writes one card a pick for every family on every index into a table of its own, a night run again replacing its own rows. Line 1, trend and strength, draws the family's gates as stored, the S&P 500's with their values and the 400's and 600's rule in words. Line 2, the business, reads 15.2's stored readings and warns at four quarters' profit at or under nothing, interest cover under 2 or a deteriorating state, and where a reading is not held, saying what is missing. Line 3, the market and the sector, reads the index's breadth against its floor and the stock's sector's place among the index's sectors by its members' mean return over 63 sessions, read by one function in the core over the index's own members' stored bars, and warns at a sector in the bottom 3 of 11 (Moskowitz and Grinblatt 1999). Line 4, earnings, reads the next report in the calendar, its reaction session taken by the earnings rule's own function, warns inside the time three in four of the rule's trades had ended by, notes a report inside the cap alone, and warns where no date is on file. Line 5, liquidity and cost, reads the 50 sessions' dollar volume and the round trip at the published table in risks, in percent for a rule with no stop, and warns at 0.10 risks or more. The values sit in one settings block with defaults in code, each stated in the card's own words, and each card stores the values it was read with. A command replays each rule a card can name, each family's live or provisional rule on each index, at its one setting over the history the pulls stored, with the sweep's own walk, each trade after its cost at the published table, and stores the history's first and last session, whether membership was as it stood or survivors only, the trades, the share won, none for a trailing rule, the average result in risks, in percent for the heavyweights, the median and three-in-four sessions held and the median worst close before the end in risks; the remedy runs it, as each later freeze's remedy does. The card opens in place under each pick's row on Tonight and at the top of the stock's page on a night it is listed, one column under 640 pixels. It draws the rule's record under the rule's own heading with the line that it is the rule's record over every stock it bought and not this stock's chance, the line that the settings were chosen on the same history so the figures read high, and "survivors only" where it applies; a rule with no record draws the dashed outline with why, and one under the rules' threshold only its count against it. Writes section 15.18 "The decision card", what it is for and each part with its source and its reason; rows in sections 7, 14, 16, 17 and 18, the failure rows a rule with no record, no report date on file, a member the night read nothing for and a rule with no stop; `SCHEMA.md`; the decisions it cites; section 23's two sources; the runbook; and CLAUDE.md's hard rule on where a record is shown with the decision behind it, superseded, the prior text in `CHANGELOG.md` and its citations moved.

**Done when** each of lines 1 to 5 is worked by hand on both sides of its threshold over constructed stores; a test changes each value in the settings block and finds its line's verdict and words changed; a report one session inside the time three in four of the rule's trades had ended by gives a warning, one a session past it and inside the cap a note, and one a session past the cap a tick; a card for an S&P 400 pick reads the S&P 400's breadth and its own sector ranks; the record's trades, share won and average result before costs equal the sweep's own reading of one setting over a constructed history, the expectation derived independently; the cards are read back off the rendered page against the store on every index, at a wide screen and at a phone's width; the fixture carries the card's expectations, the second, third and fourth lines' worked by hand; and the entry states the pin lists read and that no pin moved.

### 16.2 The plan in the operator's money, the settings page and the Taken control
The account size, the risk a trade in per cent and the position cap, proposed at a fifth of the account, are kept in `account.json` under the data root, written whole by a small settings page reached from the masthead, a press without the page's own header refused as every press is (see: The account settings live in a file of their own under the data root and in nothing the store or the logs hold). One function in the core works out the plan where the card is drawn: the shares, the dollars at risk over the stop's distance rounded down and no more than the position cap allows; the dollars at risk; the position's value and its share of the account; and the round trip in dollars at the published table. A holding with no stop is sized as ruled, and unset settings draw the plan in prices and risks with one line linking the page. The management is the rule's own, written from the family and the stored row: the pullback's stop, target and cap; the breakout's stop raised to the highest close less the plan's distance and never lowered, with no target; the drift's stop, target and cap; the heavyweights held to a month's first close where the rule would not buy them; and the S&P 400's and 600's rules from their stored cap, target and trail. Each stop is stated as a close below it, and no step any rule lacks is named. A Taken control on each card records a taken trade with an optional fill price and date, the date the session after the pick's night and its fill as ruled (see: A taken trade's fill is the next session's open once its bar is stored, and the plan's buy marked provisional until then), refusing a fill at or under the stop, a stock already holding an open taken trade and a press without the header; "Not taken" removes a row only before its first night follows it, and after that an exit is recorded with a price and a date. Line 6 counts the operator's open taken trades in the stock's sector across all three indices and warns at 2 or more. An exported report carries no part of the card that reads the account settings or the taken trades. Writes the decision superseding the one that a screen computes nothing, with its one exception and its 46 citations moved; the decision that the concentration line reads every index, narrowing the one that every figure names its index; `store-never-deleted`'s list gaining the settings file written whole; sections 15.18, 16, 17 and 18; `SCHEMA.md`; the runbook; and the guide.

**Done when** shares are worked by hand rounded down, an account of 10,000 at a risk of 1 per cent buying at 50.00 with a stop at 47.30 taking 37 shares and 99.90 at risk, and capped, the stop at 49.50 taking 200 by the risk and 40 by the cap of a fifth; each family's management equals its rule's on every index and names no step the rule lacks; line 6 is worked by hand on both sides of 2, an open trade on another index counted; a press without the header writes nothing and a fill at or under the stop is refused; and settings with distinctive values are found in no byte of the store, no captured log line, no run log row and no export, the file's path under the data root that `.gitignore` covers.

### 16.3 What could hit it, and the operator's record
The earnings line gains the stock's stored reactions: the median move in typical moves and in the plan's risks, and how many moved further than the stop's distance. The night asks the provider's dividend calendar for each of the next 21 sessions, one request a session, and stores each declared ex-date in the calendar under a kind of its own; the quarters fetch keeps the dividend part of the answer it already asks for, the forward rate, the last ex-date and each year's count of dividends; and the card draws the next ex-date inside the hold, declared where the calendar carries one and estimated as the company's last declared ex-date plus its usual interval where it does not, with the amount in risks from the forward rate over the year's count. Each FOMC decision and CPI release inside the hold is read from `market-events.json` at the root, each row naming the page it was read from, and a hold running past the table's last date says so (see: Market events inside a hold are read from a committed table of the Fed's and the BLS's own dates, and asked of no provider). A night step after the S&P 400's and 600's families follows each open taken trade over the closes after its fill under its rule's own management, the trail's distance the plan's and not the fill's and the cap counted from the fill; it ends a trade at its stop, its target, its cap, the book's own sale for a heavyweight, an exit the operator recorded or the stock leaving every index, writes where and why with the result in risks from the fill, replaces a provisional fill with the stored open, and reads prices at the series' scale on the night so a split inside a trade ends nothing. The operator's record per family and index, won, lost and open with its average result as ruled (see: The operator's own record states its average result once twenty of its trades in a family and index have ended), is drawn under the rule's heading beside the rule's record and beside the rule's own listed trades on the same nights; Past picks gains "Your trades" for the chosen index, open ones first, each with its exit control. No list, pick, queue, report request or record reads the taken trades, held by a check over every component that makes a pick (owes: The card and the follower read within their time on the first five nights). Writes the decision for the night's ex-dividend ask with CLAUDE.md's hard rule naming it, the prior text in `CHANGELOG.md`; sections 7, 14, 15.18, 16, 17 and 18; section 23's source; `SCHEMA.md`; the runbook; and the guide.

**Done when** a taken trade is followed by hand to its stop, to its target, to its cap, along a raised trail, through a split and to an exit the operator recorded, and a close equal to its stop leaves it open; a provisional fill is replaced by the stored open, an entered price is never replaced and an open under the stop is read at the plan's distance; the record per family and index is worked by hand on each index, under the twenty and at it; a declared ex-date and an estimated one are each worked by hand, a payer the calendar carries no date for drawing the estimate and one it carries drawing the declared date; a hold crossing the events table's end says so; the reactions are read back in the plan's risks against figures worked by hand; and the nightly cost check holds the night's requests at the figure stated.

### 16.4 The guide, the figure, the screens read back, and the phase report
One SVG figure of the card with a plain key beneath explaining every part, its figures drawn as n.n and nn% rather than any day's, in section 15.18 and in the guide's new "How to read a pick's card", written for a reader who does not invest; screenshots of the card on each index at a wide screen and at a phone's width, read back against the store; and `tools/verify-phase` reaching every claim the phase added, with the pair predicted above checked against the actual, every claim that moved named and the phase's operating row named in its report.

**Done when** the phase report is green with no claim unexamined, the pair is checked against the actual, the figure's key names every part the figure draws, and every screenshot is read back against the store.

### 16.5 The news labeller waits for the end of a peak window
On the operator's ruling of 2026-10-07, after the report and inside phase 16. The labeller the night starts after its close stops at once where a peak window of its profile is open, and on the night of 2026-10-06 it started at 01:35 UTC inside the window from 01:00 to 04:00 and labelled nothing. It waits instead for the window to end before each call where one is open, as the drain does, its time limit counted over the time it labels and not the time it waits; and the store's copy waits for it until the latest instant a labeller started with it could end, its limit counted the same way, and ten minutes more (see: The news labeller waits for the end of a peak window rather than stopping at one, and its time limit counts the time it labels). Writes the decision, section 18's row for a peak window opening while the labeller runs, the labeller's and the copy's catalogue rows, the runbook's labeller and copy paragraphs, the roster's `fixture-expectations` clauses, and the guide where it says when the labeller runs.

**Done when** a labeller started inside a peak window, over a clock its own wait moves, waits to the window's end and labels; a retry reaching a window waits for its end and is asked; one started a second before a window's end waits that second and one started at its end waits none; a run whose labelling time reaches its limit stops with its waits left out of the count; the latest end a copy waits for is worked by hand for a start inside a window, one outside it and one whose limit runs into the next window; and the entry names its rows beside phase 16's report.

## Phase 17: the improvement loop, from diagnosis to a monthly decision

**Visible output at 17.2.** Every setup card on every index gains a selector beside its heading, "Live rule" by default and then each registered variant by number and name, redrawing the card as the chosen rule sees tonight under a band saying its picks are not tonight's list; a line beneath each card saying how many nights the chosen rule has listed nothing and how often a stretch that long has occurred in its own history; and under each breakout card a list of the members closest to breaking out, with the price each would have to close above and the volume the rule would need.

**What this phase is for.** The operator's brief of 2026-10-08: the loop measures but cannot change anything on a useful timescale. Each rule's first look comes at eight blocks of 63 sessions, about two years, with no promotion before twelve, every registration again restarts a rule's record where its replay differs, every search so far has turned the same few dials, and nothing asks why trades lose. The phase adds diagnosis, proposals with reasons, tests on years the proposal never saw, and a monthly decision by the operator: a ledger of every setup a family's loose gates pass on each index with its readings as they stood and how it turned out; three engines proposing from it, an autopsy of finished trades proposing exits, a comparison of winners and losers proposing entry conditions, and a fitted score; a walk-forward tester judging each proposal per family per index on years it never read; a monthly run putting at most one proposal a family an index to the operator on a Loop page; and a fifth family starting from the business. Rules choose the stocks, the fitted score is a rule with fixed code and a fixed seed, no language model's output enters any pick, condition, ranking or test, the operator's taken trades feed nothing, every reading is as it stood on its session with membership as it stood, nothing is judged on the data it was built from, no engine sets aside, retires or hides any family, and no paid model call and no purchase is made (see: Every change to a rule waits for the operator's approval on the Loop page, and the automatic mode ships off) (see: Each family on each index is proposed for, tested on and approved separately, on its own index's history alone) (see: A change is adopted only on test years the proposal never saw, and a search is judged as a procedure run year by year) (see: The operator's taken trades never feed a pick, a rule, an engine or a test).

**What the evaluation found.** The code, the corpus and the live store, opened read-only, were read against the brief on 2026-10-08, with the sweeps' own output files and the published evidence behind each idea. Most of the brief stands; seven parts of it ran into standing rules, the store's contents or measured limits, and each changes the design.
- Part 0's searches cannot become candidates in the first monthly run as fixed settings: every sweep that chose a live or provisional setting, and every Part 0 search, reads 2019-01-02 to the newest pulled session, so a setting it chose was built from the very years the tester would judge it on. The tester judges each search as a procedure instead, run on the years before each test year and scored on that year, and the proposal put to the operator is the procedure run on all finished data; the incumbent carries the same bias the other way, chosen on the test years, and the Loop page says so.
- The S&P 400 drift's extra condition is met by no setting read so far: none of its 81 grid settings and none of its 170 second-stage tries holding 300 trades is positive over 2025 and 2026 together, the best at a loss of 0.053 and 0.015 risks a trade. The search runs and says so. Its revenue dial reads revenue as known from the report date, both quarters as the first filing states them (see: A drift print's revenue growth is its quarter's revenue as first filed against the same quarter a year before), and first-filed revenue covers 130 of 736 S&P 400 filers and 77 of 1,137 S&P 600 filers, so a free pull of the SEC's company facts for about 2,166 filers comes first, about 25 minutes and no provider call.
- The S&P 400 pullback with profit and cover is thinner than the brief states: 554 trades over eight years at 0.218 risks a trade, 33 trades in 2020 and 2022, listing on 20.4 per cent of nights against the search's plateau floor of 60, the tenth of ten crossed settings and the only one meeting the floors, and reading market 50 per cent, dry-up off and a stop of 0.5 to 2.5 moves that the brief does not name. Its half-step neighbourhood is read under the family floors, naming the dial and direction that break it.
- The breakout has no trend or strength gate on any index: its gates are the market, a new high, volume, a tightened range and the trade. On the S&P 400 and 600 a reading is made only for a member already closing above the 126-session high on at least 1.25 times its volume, so a member forming a breakout has no reading, and the range ratio is not stored. The forming list reads the rule's own gates, and its closing line says the rule reads its other gates that night as on any other and lists five at most, since the line as written would be false where the market check is closed, where five are listed, where an open trade holds the stock or where the range sits between the rule's ceiling and 1.0.
- The bar store drops its oldest session every night, so a path older than a year could not be rebuilt from bars, and the brief is right that paths must be kept; but one path a setup is the wrong unit. At about 150,000 setups a year, 63 sessions of high, low and close is about 1.5 kilobytes a setup, about 225 megabytes a year and 1.7 gigabytes for the history, more than the store holds today, and the edge subtracts the same plan on every member that night, which per-setup paths cannot replay under another exit. The bars the fetcher drops are kept instead in a table of their own that no night reads, about 58 megabytes a year, each setup stores its anchor, and any exit replays from bars through one function, for a setup and for every night's benchmark alike.
- The brief's pass rule lets a null proposal through about one time in six, since better in 4 of 5 periods including the latest holds under the null with probability 5 in 32; no heavyweights rule can reach 300 test trades, the S&P 500's holding 226 over 2022 to 2026 and the 400's and 600's 298 and 279; and a correction counted over every run so far rises without end, a one-sided bar of 2.71 standard errors at the first run and 3.86 at the sixtieth. The pass rule is a block sign-flip test of the paired difference in total edge after costs over 63-session blocks, a Romano-Wolf step-down across the proposals tested for the rule in the run, held to a fixed bar of 0.05 over 12 a run so the expected false approvals a rule a year stay at 0.05 under any dependence, with a stability screen of 60 per cent of the complete test years holding 50 trades a side, the five largest absolute results removed from each side, and the heavyweights judged on 36 monthly book returns in place of 300 trades (see: A proposal passes the tester on a block sign-flip test of its total edge after costs against the current rule, corrected within a run and held to a fixed bar across runs).
- LightGBM ships no native library for the macOS runner's architecture, and FastTree runs managed code there and native code on the operator's machine, so a model trained on one platform is not the same model on the other. The learned score is ridge regression written in the core, deterministic with no package and no seed, and FastTree is its challenger on one thread with a fixed seed and a seeded row subsample, adopted only where it beats ridge on the same folds; the label is the edge in typical moves winsorised at the learning window's 1st and 99th percentiles, since in risks it carries a kurtosis of 9.9 over the S&P 500's loose setups.
- A card's record of setups like tonight's pick runs into the hard rule that a card's record is the rule's over every stock it bought and not this stock's chance. The card keeps the rule's record and adds a part of its own under the rule's heading, the 250 nearest finished setups of the same family and index on percentile ranks of the six readings the index's score weighs most, tonight's ticker excluded and one setup a ticker a 21-session window, drawn with its count, its mean edge and a 90 per cent interval, and reading "Not distinguishable from the rule's record" beside it where the interval holds the rule's own mean; at 250 neighbours the interval is about 0.25 to 0.36 risks wide, wider than most rules' edge.
- An approval cannot go through the remedy script, which builds and runs the working tree, so a remedy run while a branch is out registers under the branch's pins and the night then skips the rule as moved; and the read surface may not reference the worker. The Loop page's press writes a decision row, and a worker verb started from the night's clean build applies it before the next night, as the Taken control's and the report press's writes are made.
- Liveness is a name prefix, at most nine rules stand a family, the S&P 400's and 600's provisional settings are code constants every index rule pins, an index family is frozen once on grid values only, and the S&P 500 pullback changes only through the shape command. Each is narrowed where the approval is built, so an approved change is a stored setting and never a code change, a replaced rule keeps its record as a variant for 63 sessions and can be restored, and a family standing at nine names the variant an approval replaces.
- Inserting a new top-level section would renumber sections 14 to 23 and every key the claims harness holds, so the loop is written as subsections of "How the picks improve".
- The night's three-index arithmetic reaches the close in 1,588 to 2,109 seconds against the wall clock of 2,400, and the store grows 44 to 47 megabytes a night against the 25 proposed. The checkpoint that adds the cards' stages measures its time over the live store before it merges, and the store's growth is measured at 17.0 and changed as its own checkpoint once the operator approves the proposal.

**What the measurement found, on 2026-10-08.** The store was opened read-only beside its copy of 2026-10-07 05:16 UTC, and each table's rows added since and its newest rows' bytes read. The night of 2026-10-07 added, in payload: the facts files 23.2 megabytes, 1,501 at 15.5 kilobytes, which the retention empties the night after so they stand as a peak and not as growth; the news articles 9.7, 2,220 at 4.4 kilobytes, kept 31 days, so their growth ends on 2026-11-01 at about 300 megabytes; the breakout's and the drift's results 4.3, 1,006 rows at 4.3 kilobytes of gate words and shadow verdicts; the levels 3.8 and the volume profiles 1.9, each kept over 23 nights by its own deleter; the swing filter's gate results 2.2, 503 rows at 4.5 kilobytes; the fundamental readings 1.6, 1,505 rows at 1.1 kilobytes; the run log 1.3, the drain's and the labeller's call rows; the version scores 1.0; and the research documents 2.0. The persistent payload is about 29 megabytes a night with the indexes and pages above it, and about 20 once the news window is full. The copy of 1.42 gigabytes took 287 seconds, about 5 megabytes a second, so a store of 9.5 gigabytes a year on takes about 32 minutes. The proposal is in 17.0's entry, for the operator's approval before 17.2a is built.

**The rulings of 2026-10-08.** The operator approved the plan in two rounds, each word for word in `prompts/`: R1 to R11 and R13 as recommended, R12 changed so a declined proposal is put again once a new complete block of 63 sessions has been added since the decline and it passes with that block included (see: A declined proposal is put again once a new complete block has been added since the decline and it passes with that block); the 15.3 correction's go and OpenFIGI without a key; five changes, the backups corrected before the night of 2026-10-08 as 13.10's correction, the one-year small-against-large reading filled from the market series as 15.2's correction, revenue known from the report date, the card's part saying where it cannot be told from the rule's record, and the store's growth and the night's headroom measured and proposed; and four more, the SEC company facts refreshed each night for every member whose filings moved, from the SEC's submissions feed, free, with the monthly run's whole refresh as the catch-all; the card's score rank shown only for a score that has passed the tester on that index; 17.5, 17.6 and 17.8 among the checkpoints that change the night's code, with every engine's settings hooks landing together at 17.5 under one `register --moved` remedy so the families' pins move once; and the growth change built as its own checkpoint before 17.3 once approved. The sign-offs of phases 15 and 16 are owed to fresh sessions the operator starts, one at a time and never beside the build, and phase 17 pauses with the checkout on main whenever one starts (see: The sign-offs of phases 15 and 16 run between phase 17's pull requests, never beside one). Each pull request merges without a go once its gates and the hosted runs are green. No paid call is made and nothing is bought; provider calls are stated before each run and held under 5,000 a day.

**Where the code lives.** The ledger's readings, the replay from bars, the folds, the pass rule, the ridge model and the alarm's bootstrap live in files of their own in the core, calling the rules' own functions, so no pin moves for them; the engines, the tester, the monthly run and the apply verb in the worker; the Ledger and Loop pages in the read surface and the pages. Part 0's searches live in the sweep's unpinned files. The one pin move is 17.5's, where the exit menu, the conditions and the score's order and threshold join the families' walks together, all off.

**One pull request a checkpoint.** 17.0, then 15.2's correction, then 17.1a and 17.1b, 17.2, 17.2a once the operator approves the growth proposal, and 17.3 to 17.10 in order, each reported after it merges. A checkpoint that changes the night's code, 17.2, 17.2a, 17.3, 17.5, 17.6, 17.7, 17.8 and 17.9, merges outside the night's window with its remedy run, and its first night is read the next morning before the next such checkpoint merges.

**What is the operator's.** Every approval and decline on the Loop page. The growth proposal's approval. Any freeze and any sweep result's reading. A provider run that would pass 5,000 calls in a day. The automatic mode's setting, never switched without their word. The sign-offs of phases 15 and 16.

**The claims phase 17 predicts.** 1,036 claims and 1,036 PASS before it. After 17.10 the pair is 1,154 and 1,154, within 1,115 to 1,195: none at 17.0, which adds section 20's row and no row of a table the harness reads; about 6 at 17.1, the searches' limits, the provider stop and the membership failure rows; about 20 at 17.2, the cards' parts, two stages, three stores and their limits; about 3 at 17.2a; about 22 at 17.3, the ledger's stores and matrix rows, two night steps, the loose gates, the failure rows and the Ledger page's parts; about 12 at 17.4; about 8 at 17.5; about 5 at 17.6; about 12 at 17.7; about 12 at 17.8; about 14 at 17.9; and about 4 at 17.10, the monthly run's rows and the figures' keys.

### 17.0 Planning
Writes this section, the decisions the phase rests on that describe no running code, section 20's row for phase 17 and the phase's carried obligations, and measures the store's growth by table and the night's time against its limit with phase 17's additions projected, bringing the growth proposal and, where the night would pass 90 per cent of its limit, the options to the operator in its entry. The decisions that change what runs are written where they are built: the variant's picks on its card and the forming list at 17.2, the kept bars and the setups replayed from them at 17.3, the fitted model as a rule at 17.7, and the narrowings of the register, the records and the shape command at 17.9, each superseding or narrowing the entry it changes. Builds none of the loop.

**Done when** every decision the checkpoints below cite resolves, no done condition below waits on the calendar, each checkpoint below names the parts of the corpus it writes, section 20 carries one row for every phase this plan holds, and the growth by table and the night's projection are recorded with the proposal.

### 17.1 Part 0, the focused searches and the history fixes
Two pull requests. The first, membership: the funds' sixty filings read again from the SEC, free, to store each holding's shares and value; the 15.3 correction, holding a name match the recovery makes to the 5 per cent level against the close at each quarter as a first match is, on the operator's go; what became of each unmatched company from the SEC's submissions per filer; about 27 coded keys mapped through OpenFIGI without a key, the client the twentieth file allowed one, their closes asked under the tracking and level checks and their history pulled by name for each code that passes, at most about 1,000 weighted calls stated before the run; the two codeless filings' rows joined to the next coded quarter's holding of the same company where the names are identical and the in-step check holds across them; and every as-it-stood search and index record run again, the entry stating what moved. The second, the searches: the SEC's revenue facts pulled for every filer the history holds, each figure as first filed with its accession number and filing date, the other five kinds the ledger reads, gross profit, operating income, operating cash flow, net income and interest expense, pulled once into their own table at 17.3 with the night step that refreshes it, where their first reader is; the index funds pulled again; then the five searches as the plan states them, each report with its figures by year, its depth and neighbours, the luck figure for its tries and what could be tried next, the drift's extra condition read, the pullback's half steps naming the dial that breaks it, the S&P 400 and 600 searched together and confirmed apart, the S&P 600 pullback's grid widened to a depth of 16 and a freshness of 21 with the binding dial printed, the breakouts' close in the top quarter of the range and entry on the first pullback to the level, and the heavyweights' six settings a design pre-registered in a ruling entry before the run with their evidence. Nothing is frozen and every family keeps listing provisional picks (see: No family on any index is set aside or hidden by a test result without the operator's word). Writes the runbook's verbs, section 17's rows for the searches' settings and the provider stop, section 18's rows for a mapping the provider's list cannot join and a pull reaching the stop, the roster's clauses, `SCHEMA.md` for the holdings' columns, and the 15.3 correction's decision with its prior text in `CHANGELOG.md`.

**Done when** the level test refuses a hand-built match whose ratio holds steady at a level off the close and keeps one at the level; the carry-back joins XPO's quarter of 2019-03-31 and leaves Commerce Bancshares as it stood; OpenFIGI's answers are captured into the fixture before their reader is written; each search's floors and luck figure are worked by hand over a constructed history; the drift's extra condition is worked on both sides; the heavyweights' six settings stand in the entry before the run; and every provider call is stated before and counted after.

### 17.3 The setup ledger, the kept bars and the filings refreshed each night
Every member-session a family's loose gates pass on an index is a setup, the gates stated here with why each is that wide: the pullback at a strength of at least 0.20, a depth of 0.5 to 12 typical moves, a freshness of at most 14, a reward to risk of at least 0.5, any band strength, dry-up or market, a price of at least $5 and half the index's dollar floor, the profit gate stored and not required; the breakout at a close above its 63-session high on at least its average volume with a range ratio of at most 1.25 and any market; the drift one setup a report with a positive surprise and a reaction of at least 0.25 typical moves, anchored at the first entry night; the heavyweights each member of its sector's size cut at each month's rebalance, capped at 252 sessions; each a grid step beyond the widest level any search tried, and the pullback's strength floor rising a step until the history fits 0.5 gigabytes. Each setup stores its identity, the live rule's name that night, whether it was picked, its plan as prices, its risk in typical moves, 36 readings each defined once in the core and read as it stood, the result, cost, edge, end and reason under the live exits, its source and the readings' definition pin; each family's night stores the benchmark under the live exits; the bars the fetcher drops land in a table of their own in the same transaction, append-only, read by no night, by a decision written here that the kept bars are a setup's path and a setup is stored as its anchor; the SEC's facts as first filed in a table of their own; and the Ledger page's counts in a summary the writers refresh. The history build runs chunked and resumable from a clean copy of main's commit, waiting for the night and the copy and holding the drain's lock, over the pulled bars merged with the store's on membership as it stood; the night step after the families appends tonight's setups and closes the windows that ended; and, on the operator's ruling of 2026-10-09, the night step after the quarters fetch reads the archive's daily index for the days since it last read one and asks the facts of the members that filed a report, an amendment or a results announcement alone, free and under its own limit, named as the seventh carve-out of the nightly hard rule with a decision written here, that the night refreshes the facts of the members that filed since its last read of the archive's daily index. The point-in-time check rebuilds a seeded sample of setups from a scratch store holding only what stood on each session. The Ledger page draws, under Universe, each family's setups a year and the share picked, the deciles of result and edge, and one chosen setup's bars with its plan's lines. The two steps' time and the ledger's growth are read over the first five nights after (owes: The night's added time over phase 17's first five nights) (owes: The ledger's growth over the five nights after it lands). Writes section 13.10 and 13.11 with the vocabulary and the nightly half's figure, the stores and matrix rows, section 14's two steps, section 17's gates and limits, section 18's rows for a missing reading, a filing the feed does not serve and a build that stops, `SCHEMA.md`, CLAUDE.md's hard rule with its prior text in `CHANGELOG.md`, the runbook and the roster's clauses.

**Done when** a reading is worked by hand as it stood against a scratch store and a reading one session ahead is refused; a setup's edge is worked by hand against the benchmark; a dropped bar lands in the kept bars in the fetcher's own transaction; a filing the archive's index lists for a session is refreshed on the next session's night and read by that night's readings, and a member that filed nothing is not asked for; the history build's setup count a family an index, its time and its size are recorded against the budget; and the night's two steps' times are recorded against the limit.

The old loop's decisions are reconciled: the block test stays the long-run record, the looks and the futility guideline become monitoring with a futility result a proposal, the shape acceptance's restarts and the proposer's one setting a gate superseded for loop changes, the clock's restart narrowed to the registered rule, the backfill and exploration decisions narrowed to history reaching the list through a walk-forward test and approval, and the record's replay and the frozen-once decisions narrowed, each moved with its reasoning. Writes the decisions, the stores, the catalogue and matrix rows, section 17's rows for the bar, the setting and the alarm, section 18's rows for an approval that cannot be applied, an alarm on a rule and a monthly run that fails, `SCHEMA.md`, CLAUDE.md's Merge section and layout, the runbook and the roster.

The monthly run as one command from a clean copy of main's commit, refused off main, chunked and resumable, waiting for the night, the drain and the copy and holding the drain's lock, its report under the data root and the Loop page's rows, at most one proposal a family an index put to the operator; `tools/monthly` and its PowerShell wrapper with the scheduler's steps for the first Saturday of the month in the runbook; the run made once by hand and reported per family per index, the scheduler's first run read when it comes (owes: The first scheduled monthly run); screenshots of the cards with a variant chosen, the Ledger and Loop pages and the decision card under each index at a wide screen and a phone's width, read back; and `tools/verify-phase` reaching every claim the phase added, the pair checked against the actual with every claim that moved named and the phase's operating rows named in its report.
